## Chapter 1 — Foundations

### Phase 1.1 — Repository and toolchain
- [x] git init, `.gitignore`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, directory skeleton
- [x] Record decisions: min macOS 26, product name, bundle id prefix, framework name
- [x] `project.yml` (app + five helper targets, entitlements, framework copy/sign phase, local packages)
- [x] `scripts/bootstrap-cef.sh` (pinned CEF 154.0.32 arm64 minimal, SHA check, CMake wrapper build, cefsimple run)
- [x] Chromium checkout at `~/chromium` on tag 154.0.8037.58; `Engine/roomcore/args/dev.gn`; first component build started; `research/notes/build-log.md`
- [x] CI stub (compile-only `xcodebuild`)
- [x] Exit: `xcodegen && xcodebuild` builds `Room.app` with helpers + CEF framework; launches to an empty window; quits cleanly. Chromium checkout complete, first build finished or ETA logged.

**Phase 1.1 done (2026-10-02).** Review: the app shell, five helpers, CEF embedding and orderly `CefShutdown` work (quit 0.23–0.33 s, no leftover helpers, no crash reports over 8 runs); the no-engine CI spec builds with the CEF dist absent. The checkout is pinned to the exact Chromium commit CEF 154.0.32 uses, and the first `chrome` component build finished. Two spec items were wrong for M154/Xcode 27 (`safe_browsing_mode=0`, missing Metal toolchain) and are recorded below. The big surprise is build cost: 4 h 24 min clean vs. the 1.3–2 h estimate (partly self-inflicted via nice +5); incrementals are fine (27–39 s). Re-measure a clean build at normal priority before trusting rebase-cost estimates in Phase 1.5/1.6.

### Phase 1.2 — The `Engine` protocol
- [x] `Packages/Engine` protocols and value types (§1.2 of ROADMAP)
- [x] `FakeEngine` + tests
- [x] `docs/engine-api.md` mapping table (Engine ↔ CEF ↔ Chromium/ArcCore)
- [x] Exit: compiles under Swift 6 strict concurrency; tests pass; mapping reviewed

**Phase 1.2 done (2026-10-02).** Review: the `Engine` package now has the full Chapter 1 surface (application/profiles/features, `Profile` with nil-able Chrome-only capabilities, `@Observable` `WebContents`, `DevTools`, UI/navigation/downloads delegates, value types), modeled on ArcCore's shape but in Swift idiom. It builds clean under Swift 6 with `-warnings-as-errors -strict-concurrency=complete`; `FakeEngine` passes 53 Swift Testing tests via `swift test`, the Room scheme's test action and the CI spec. `docs/engine-api.md` maps all ~83 members to CEF and Chromium; a row-by-row check against the headers and the M154 checkout fixed 19 entries, and the CEF gap list (7 items, mostly emulatable over JavaScript/CDP) is the input for Phase 1.4. Mapping doc read and accepted (planner, 2026-10-02); spot checks against `cef_download_item.h`, `cef_audio_handler.h` and `alloy_browser_host_impl.cc` agree. The 1.4 findings are now folded into `ROADMAP.md` Phase 1.4 (tasks 1–3, three new spike items).

**Phase 1.3 done (2026-10-02).** Review: the window, material, space tint, sidebar placeholder with snap-closing splitter and the clipped/bordered/shadowed card are built in `Design`, `Card` and `UIKitCore`, with no engine work; `Room.app` now opens this window. Measured against Dia's capture at 1× (`research/notes/spike-results.md`), card edges, corner and title position match to the pixel and the shadow within 3 levels; the remaining visible difference is material brightness, which comes from what sits behind the window. Two ROADMAP guesses were wrong (inset 8 → 6, radius 12 → 10), and Dia's traffic-light position turned out to come from an empty unified toolbar, not from moving the buttons. 90 package tests pass (37 new, including 10 window-state captures with pixel checks); screenshots of all states and the Dia comparisons are in `research/screenshots/phase-1.3/`.

### Phase 1.4 — CEF inside the card and the spike checklist
- [x] Bootstrap (NSApplication subclass with CefAppProtocol, main.swift, helper main, sandbox)
- [x] `CEFClient` handlers → Engine delegates
- [x] `CEFWebContents` with `SetAsChild` into the card; hidden browsers; DevTools pane
- [x] Throwaway debug toolbar
- [x] Spike checklist recorded in `research/notes/spike-results.md` (clipping, resize, overlay, keyboard, popups, DevTools, memory, extensions, autofill dropdown, signing+notarize, shutdown)
- [x] Exit: all items recorded; page-in-card screenshot matches Dia chrome; every red item has a fallback

**Phase 1.4 done (2026-10-02).** Review: real pages render in the clipped, bordered card through the `Engine` protocol (`EngineCEF`: an ObjC++ `RoomCEFBrowser` bridge with one `CefClient` forwarding every handler, plus `CEFProfile`/`CEFWebContents`/`CEFDevTools` in Swift); card edges and corner match Dia to the pixel with a page in it, and the card fill tracks the page's root background. All 14 spike items have numbers: 11 green, 3 amber with fallbacks (IOSurface scan-out unmeasured without Quartz Debug; notarization blocked on a credential profile only the user can create; Web Audio invisible to the audibility poll), none red. The surprises were CEF-on-macOS behaviours the research did not predict: `ShowDevTools` into a view crashes (docked DevTools is now our own front-end browser over the debugging port), `DoClose`→`false` closes the whole host window, profiles must be direct children of the root cache path, CDP results can arrive synchronously, and own-window captures cannot see Chromium's layer. Shutdown with 10 pages is 0.54–0.66 s even at load 35–40; hidden browsers cost ~190–330 MB RSS each.

### Phase 1.5 — RoomCore embryo
- [x] Fork layout (branch `room`, `//room/` symlink, patches/, chromium_src/, scripts)
- [x] Branding/build with our names; `chrome` target builds
- [x] `//room/core` ObjC API: RoomApplication, RoomProfile, RoomWebContents, RoomBrowser + stub RoomBrowserWindow; factory patch; `AsBrowserView()` count
- [x] Swift host executable driving the framework; helpers
- [x] Packaging script; ad-hoc signed `Room.app` from the fork
- [x] Measurements in build-log
- [x] Exit: page renders in the Phase 1.3 window from RoomCore; clean quit

**Phase 1.5 done (2026-10-03).** Review: our Chromium fork builds as `Room.app`/`RoomCore.framework` (M154, 5 small patches, everything else in `//room`) and a Swift host drives it through an Objective-C API of our own: Wikipedia renders in the Phase 1.3 card (`research/screenshots/phase-1.5-roomcore-window.jpg`), navigation state flows back through KVO, real mouse and scroll input work, and ten pages quit in 0.18–0.55 s with exit 0, no helpers left and no crash reports. The clean build on the 24 GB Mac mini took 4 h 15 min, but only at 6 jobs; at 10 it swapped (PLAN §4 amended). First contact with a non-Views `BrowserWindow` produced ten failures, each small and fixed in `//room` except one patch (list in `spike-results.md`): Chrome reaches into the window for a location bar, an exclusive-access context, the main menu's Bookmarks item and feature teardown, and it CHECKs instead of tolerating their absence. Over ten sites Chrome called only 12 of the 68 stubbed `BrowserWindow` members. The surprise was the keychain: with ad-hoc builds every navigation silently stalls behind a login-password prompt for "Room Safe Storage", so ad-hoc RoomCore builds use the mock keychain. Still open: the team-signed keychain check and notarization (both need the user), unrequested Google network calls at launch, and a burst of TLS handshake errors that do not affect page loads.

**Phase 1.6 done (2026-10-03).** Review: `research/notes/chapter-1-review.md` answers Chapter 1's three unknowns: clipping is pixel-exact at 120 fps; a stub-`BrowserWindow` fork is drivable from Swift at about a day of first-contact fixes; a clean build takes 4+ h. It sums up 15 spike items (12 green, 3 amber with fallbacks), both machines' build timings and the memory numbers, lists what surprised us, and re-ranks the risks: rebase/build cost is now first, and clipping, overlay z-order and traffic lights are retired. The engine plan is confirmed. PLAN §4 gains Keychain, Network calls and Main menu rows, §7 the new ranking. Tokens v0 (`docs/design-tokens.md`) and the shortcut table (`docs/shortcuts.md`) are frozen; the reserved set shrank to 16 escape-hatch shortcuts in `UIKitCore` with tests, so ⌘S and ⌘, now go to the page first. Chapter 2 is specified phase by phase in `ROADMAP.md`, with the Chapter 1 carry-overs and the CEF caps placed, and `docs/backlog.md` holds the 13 loose ends. Not done: the IOSurface composite measurement, because Instruments needs Developer mode (an admin password); it is backlog B-1 with the `RoundedCornersF` fallback kept.

**Chapter 1 done (2026-10-03).** Phases 1.1–1.6 closed; PLAN §4 updated; Chapter 2 spec written.

## Chapter 2 — The sidebar browser

Spec: `ROADMAP.md` Chapter 2 (detailed in Phase 1.6). One line per task; exit criteria last.

### Phase 2.1 — Model and store
- [x] `Model` on GRDB: schema v1, migrations, one database
- [x] `Store`: `@Observable` MainActor stores as the only writers
- [x] Persistence: debounced transactional writes, generation counter, JSON snapshot, unclean-exit flag
- [x] Domain rules with tests (§A.9 conversions, Essentials cap, `isChanged`, MRU, ⌘W behaviour, folder depth, ⌘1–9 order)
- [x] Window state: sidebar width/visibility persisted (Chapter 1 carry-over)
- [x] First-launch seed
- [x] Exit: 100 random `kill -9`s, every reopen intact at the last committed generation; tests in Room scheme and CI

**Phase 2.1 done (2026-10-03).** Review: the sidebar's data now lives in `Model` (GRDB 7.11.1, schema v1 with all eleven tables incl. trigram FTS5 history, one `Room.sqlite` in Application Support) behind `Store`'s three `@Observable` stores, which are its only writers and report each mutation's touched ids instead of diffing the tree. Writes coalesce for 300 ms into one generation-numbered transaction that writes only the diff, then a JSON snapshot; a corrupt database is rebuilt from the snapshot. Two 100-kill runs (300 ms and 5 ms debounce) reopened intact every time: integrity ok, content hash equal to the generation on disk, 56 kills landed mid-transaction and all rolled back cleanly (`spike-results.md`). The kill test found one real bug, a JSON backup that could fall two generations behind, now fixed at open. 42 Model and 21 Store tests (plus 2 new window tests) run in the Room scheme and the no-engine CI spec; the app restores sidebar width/visibility and the window frame, and the window title now comes from the seeded space.

**Phase 2.2 done (2026-10-03), one check open.** Review: the whole sidebar tree is interactive against FakeEngine: rows in every §A.1 state, the §A.2 click table, menus, toasts, and in 2.2b drag and drop with conversion, the cap, drop-onto folders, ⌥-copies, outside URLs, multi-selection, keyboard focus, the overflow line, Clear's slide-out and the folder preview; every §A.2–A.9 bullet is done or named for its later phase above. Drag conversion, group moves, duplicates, drops and Clear/Undo are tested on `SidebarStore`, `DropRules`/`MultiSelection` and the controller (Sidebar 26, Store 25 tests). The captures found two real bugs the tests could not (the outline lost its last rows after the window got shorter; the focus ring ignored key state), now fixed. Open: the scroll criterion is met at 60 Hz but only proxied for 120 Hz (2–8% of 120 Hz-sized steps exceed 8.33 ms when a row enters, programmatic scroll without responsive scrolling); it needs one run on the laptop's panel.

### Phase 2.3 — Tabs and the card
- [x] `TabController`: one `WebContents` per loaded entry, card swap, background binding
- [x] Lifecycle: unloaded restore, idle and memory-pressure discard
- [x] ⌘W, ⇧⌘T, related-tab insertion, MRU on close, empty state
- [x] Switching: ⌃Tab MRU overlay, ⌘1–9, ⇧⌘]/[, ⌥⌘↓/↑
- [x] Page state into the store (title, favicon cache, live URL, changed, audible/mute)
- [x] Delete Chapter 1 debug page list (the toolbar's URL field stays until 2.4)
- [x] Exit: 50 entries/10 loaded, switching < 16 ms (median 9.4–9.5 ms, p95 14.5–15.4 ms, main thread, click to the new page committed in the card), RSS 4.1–4.2 GB summed, relaunch restores with only the selection loaded (1 page, 0.5–0.6 s to it) — measured with the session locked (occluded window); the visible-window rerun is B-18
- [x] Exit: ⌘W / ⇧⌘T / MRU per spec in tests against FakeEngine (Tabs 23 tests) and on CEF through key events (`scripts/run-spike.sh tabs`); the by-hand pass is in the list below

**Phase 2.3 done (2026-10-03).** Review: entries are real CEF pages now. The `Tabs` package's `TabController` is the sidebar's host: one page per loaded entry, created on first selection, background pages hidden, the card swapping views and following each page's background; URL, title and favicons (cached by host, so restored and discarded rows keep them) flow into the store; links opened from a page become Today tabs below it (§A.7); idle and memory-pressure discards release renderers (7 pages: 9 → 2 renderers, 1.7 → 0.8 GB). ⌘W, ⇧⌘T, the ⌃Tab switcher, ⌘1–9, ⇧⌘]/[, ⌥⌘↓/↑ and ⇧⌘M work, tested on FakeEngine and checked on CEF with key events. Switching costs 9.5 ms median / 15.4 ms p95 on the main thread after a profile found the hidden debug panel re-rendering on every switch; 10 loaded pages sum to ~4.1 GB; a relaunch loads only the selected page. Running it on CEF found three real bugs tests could not: quitting removed every Today tab (fixed with `EngineApplication.isTerminating`), shifted menu shortcuts (⇧⌘T, also 2.2's ⇧⌘D/⇧⌘K) never matched, and the switcher's highlight never showed. Open: every number was taken with the screen locked; B-18 asks for one unlocked rerun.

**Phase 2.4 done (2026-10-03).** Review: ⌘T/⌘L is one palette over our own pipeline: open tabs, pinned and essentials, history (FTS5 trigram with a decayed frecency that needs no decay job, inline host and path completion, ⇧Delete), commands (`>`, ⇥ from empty), site-search chips and Google/DuckDuckGo/Kagi suggestions behind a switch that holds at once. History is recorded through the new `didCommitNavigation` on CEF (typed, link, same-document, reload and error pages each behave right). Profiling made the palette open in 2.3 ms of our code and 6.2 ms to the next frame (2.4a on the same machine: 17.4 ms); the largest cost was AppKit's password AutoFill walking all 500 sidebar rows on every focus. Open: a query's first keystroke takes 22–25 ms because SwiftUI draws eight rows of new text at once while the glass grows — under 16 ms needs AppKit rows, a PLAN §4 UI-framework call for the user; and every number was taken at 60 Hz with screen sharing on (B-20).

### Phase 2.5 — Spaces
- [x] Space CRUD, form, context menu (form in the sidebar, SwiftUI leaf; Delete Space with an Undo toast, B-15)
- [x] Switching (icons, ⌃digits, ⌥⌘←/→, swipe, ⌃wheel, palette) with animation (250 ms slide of prepared lists, tint crossfade, Reduce Motion crossfade)
- [x] Per-space selection; essentials crossfade
- [x] Profiles as request contexts; cross-profile move; history per profile (schema v3)
- [x] Drag entry onto a space icon (drop moves it; 500 ms on the icon switches to the space)
- [x] Exit: §B minus routing (checklist in the 2.5 log); cookies separated across profiles (`scripts/spaces-cookies.sh`); no material flash on switch (mid-slide window capture); the slide does not wait on a list: 0 lists loaded in 60 switches, ⌃digit to the slide's first frame 10.9 ms median / 17.1 ms p95 at 60 Hz with 50 loaded pages over 5 spaces (`spike-results.md`)

**Phase 2.5 done (2026-10-03).** Review: spaces are real: each has its own lists, look (one to three colours through five colour-wheel algorithms, an emoji or SF Symbol) and optionally its own profile, which on CEF is its own request context at `<root>/Room Profile <name>`; cookies stay in their profile (checked on CEF), and history follows the profile (schema v3). Every space's list is built and drawn once after launch, so a switch only slides two existing lists and crossfades the tint: 6.4 ms of our code, 10.9 ms median from ⌃digit to the slide's first frame on a 60 Hz display, no list loaded during 60 switches. The surprise was where a list's cost lives: a hidden outline reloads in 0.3 ms, but its rows are made on first display, which cost the first switch to each space ~65 ms until the lists were drawn once off screen. Open: gestures (swipe, ⌃+wheel, drags) are built on pure rules with tests but only a person can try them, and every number was taken at 60 Hz with the window not key.

**Phase 2.6 done (2026-10-03).** Review: every CEF delegate callback now has Room's own UI: dialogs, auth, the certificate interstitial, permissions, menus and file choosers (2.6a); downloads with the footer ring and popover, find, zoom, print, the status pill, Copy URL, the site panel, the crashed-page view, element fullscreen and a resizable DevTools pane (2.6b). One spike run drives all 26 members on CEF through recording proxies. Running the pages found five CEF behaviours the API docs do not spell out (find's first request only counts, an alert blocks a navigation's commit on Alloy, answered permission prompts are "dismissed" too, download updates lose the name, content settings answer with defaults) and each now has a workaround in `EngineCEF` and a row in `engine-api.md`. Open: camera and microphone prompts still need a machine with devices (B-26), and the try-list below needs a person.

## Chapter 4 — A complete browser

Spec: `ROADMAP.md` Chapter 4 (written 2026-10-06). One line per task; exit criteria last. Build rule: the engine stays on 155.0.8059.36; `//room` changes only in the engine steps; no new patch; one rebase and official build at 4.9.

### Decided 2026-10-06 (user, in chat)
- [x] Chapter 4 starts before the week's notes; "3.9 week" rows are assigned at each fold-in
- [x] A DMG after each phase; migrations need not be reversible (`scripts/reset-state.sh` before an install when told)
- [x] No scheduled rebases; one only on the user's request or a phase's need
- [x] Today tabs per window; incognito in scope (a patch allowed if required); the Library in the sidebar
- [x] Import treats every browser equally; sessions make their own fixtures; nothing special-cased for any vendor (B-69 none)
- [x] B-27, B-46, B-63, B-66, B-68, B-72, B-74 keep the planner's choices
- [ ] Optional, any time: the feed URL and the EdDSA signature

**Phase 4.1 done (2026-10-06).** Review: Today tabs age into an Archive (schema v7) at a sweep every 10 min and at launch, and come back from the Library in the sidebar (⇧⌘L, ⌘Y) or the palette's History group; Clear and Delete Space go through it with their Undo, and the Archive now keeps 30 days of rows and 7 days of back/forward lists (B-80). The state database is copied (`VACUUM INTO`) at launch and every 30 min while it changes, pruned on Arc's schedule, and Help ▸ Restore Sidebar… brings a backup back in one write after backing up the present; a crash relaunches with every entry unloaded and a toast; the JSON snapshot lost its navigation states (5.76 MB → 157 KB on the worst case). The engine step added `Profile.removeDownload`, so Clear and Remove hold across a relaunch (B-59), and measured the incremental official build after a `//room` change at 6 min 44 s of build (8 min wall, the link 353 s at 8.58 GiB), well under the 45 min rule. Open: Restore needs a blank page to hold the Browser open (B-81, an EngineRoomCore fix for the next engine step); `window_main` fails while another app is frontmost (a run condition).

(5) `ARCHS=arm64` (CEF dist is arm64-only; Release builds were universal by default). (6) `EngineConfiguration.usesSystemKeychain`: ad-hoc-signed builds get a new code identity per build, so Chromium's "Chromium Safe Storage" keychain read prompted for the login password on every launch and blocked shutdown until `MacShutdownWatchdog` killed the process; builds without a Team ID now pass `--use-mock-keychain`. Signing with the author's Apple Developer team (available) in Phase 1.4 should make this unnecessary. (7) PLAN §4 build-time row updated with the measured 4 h 24 min.

CI not run on GitHub: the repo has no remote; the same commands pass locally. Long builds must be started from bash, not zsh `&` (`BG_NICE`).

App still launches and quits cleanly (smoke: exit 0, no helpers left, no crash reports).
  - Deviations / additions beyond the ROADMAP list: (1) `MenuModel` is a `@MainActor` class, not a value type, because it carries the one-shot select/cancel completion; `MenuItem` is a value type. (2) Delegates use completion handlers, not `async`, so the engine can dismiss a pending dialog/prompt itself (`webContentsDidDismissJavaScriptDialogs`, `webContentsDidDismissPermissionPrompt`), as ArcCore and CEF do. (3) Added members the CEF/Chromium mapping needs: `isReservedKeyEvent`/`handleUnhandledKeyEvent` (pre/unhandled key routing, PLAN §2.4), `webContentsDidClose`, observable `securityState` and `findResult`, `.closed` in `WebContentsState`, download pause/resume/cancel on `Profile`. (4) `features` is the startup override set (`EngineFeatures` in `EngineConfiguration`), since Chromium fixes `base::FeatureList` at startup and CEF cannot query it. (5) `WebContentsUIDelegate` members have default "no UI" implementations (dismiss/deny/cancel; unhandled keys to the main menu), standing in for ObjC `@optional`. (6) Value types are `nonisolated` + `Sendable` so they are usable off the main actor despite the package's MainActor default isolation.
  - Findings for Phase 1.4 (from the mapping review): CEF cannot make a windowed browser transparent through `background_color` (CDP override instead); `CefWindowInfo::hidden` is ignored with `SetAsChild`; `CefAudioHandler` capture mutes the speakers, but returning `false` from `GetAudioParameters` still gives a "became audible" edge; `LoadURL` always uses TYPED|FROM_ADDRESS_BAR. For Chapter 3: `SecurityStateTabHelper` is gone in M154 (`chrome_security_state::` functions), `TabHelpers::AttachTabHelpers` is friend-gated, main-world JavaScript needs CDP or a `//room` hook.
  - Observation: quit time is load-sensitive. At load average ~40–48 (Chrome, Spotlight, a parallel source scan) quits took 0.4–7.9 s and one run exceeded the smoke script's 10 s limit; the pre-phase build (360c68f) built side by side also exceeded 10 s once under the same load, so this is not a regression. Phase 1.4's "⌘Q < 1 s" spike should be measured on an idle machine and record the load average.

  - Deviations: (1) Profiles live at `<root_cache_path>/Room Profile <name>`, not `<root>/Profiles/<name>`: CEF only loads a disk profile whose `cache_path` is a direct child of the root and silently falls back to off-the-record otherwise. (2) `nativeView` is a view `CEFWebContents` owns, with CEF's view inside it (stable across the emulated discard), not CEF's view itself. (3) DevTools cannot be parented with `SetAsChild` on CEF 154 macOS (`ShowDevTools` with a parent `DCHECK`s — the crash reported mid-session); docked DevTools is our own Alloy browser loading the bundled front-end over a 127.0.0.1 remote-debugging port (Debug builds only; any local process can drive the browser through that port); Release uses CEF's separate DevTools window. (4) CEF builds always use the mock keychain: a Developer ID–signed build prompted for the login password over an existing "Chromium Safe Storage" item and CEF 154's stable API cannot rename it. This reverses the Phase 1.1 note that team signing would make the mock keychain unnecessary; RoomCore (Chapter 3) brands its own item. (5) The empty unified toolbar is hidden while in fullscreen (ROADMAP's named fallback): AppKit otherwise shows it as a 52 pt strip with the menu bar. Swift host `App/RoomCore` (target `RoomCoreHost`, scheme "Room (RoomCore)", `-RoomSpike open10|nav`) compiles against a header-only stand-in. `scripts/roomcore-{build,package}.sh`, `scripts/embed-roomcore-framework.sh`; `scripts/sign-and-notarize.sh` handles the RoomCore layout. `docs/setup.md` for new machines.

Development moves to a Mac mini: its first clean build is the clean-build measurement Phase 1.5 asks for (record the machine and load).
  - Deviations so far: (1) the framework is renamed `RoomCore.framework` in the build (patch 0001), not in packaging: helpers `dlopen` `../../../../<framework name>` and `chrome_paths_mac.mm` derives the framework path from its name, so a packaging-time rename breaks both (Arc does the same: helpers load `../../../../ArcCore`). (2) No patch to `chrome_browser_application_mac`: the host subclasses `RoomCrApplication`, a RoomCore-exported subclass of `BrowserCrApplication`, so `RegisterBrowserCrApp()` accepts the host's NSApp and Chromium keeps its `sendEvent:`/accessibility/`terminate:` behaviour; a host that only conformed to `CrAppProtocol` would crash in `NativeEventObserver` (`addNativeEventProcessorObserver:`). (3) The Swift host links RoomCore directly instead of `dlopen`ing it: Swift needs the ObjC classes at link time, Chromium's own component build links the framework from the exe, and Dia links ArcCore; without the early malloc-zone registration PartitionAlloc takes its documented single-threaded path at load time. (4) `RoomApplication.run` calls `ChromeMain` with `--no-startup-window --no-first-run --no-default-browser-check`; the delegate starts from `-[NSApplication finishLaunching]` (Chrome's AppController stays NSApp.delegate). (5) `tryToTerminate` has no completion: Chromium gives no "terminate cancelled" signal; `roomApplicationWillTerminate:` reports the point of no return. (6) The keychain item name does not follow the branding product name (hard-coded "Chromium Safe Storage" in `keychain_password_mac.mm`), hence patch 0002.
  - Finding: on M154 `AsBrowserView()` has 11 references, 1 in non-test code (`address_bubbles_controller.cc`); upstream moved to `BrowserView::GetBrowserViewForBrowser` (161 non-test call sites), which returns null for a non-Views window. `Browser`'s `BrowserWindowCreateParams::window` is test-only (`CHECK_IS_TEST`), so the factory patch is required.

  - Done: `Packages/Model` (GRDB 7.11.1 pinned exactly; `Timestamp`, `Profile`, `Space`, `Entry`, `SplitGroup`, `WindowState`, `WindowSpace`, `Favicon`, `Settings`, `RoomState`; `Schema` v1 migration; `RoomDatabase`; `StateWriter`; `RoomStorage` with snapshot recovery; rules: `URLNormalization`, `EntryRules`, `FolderRules`, `NavigationRules`); `Packages/Store` (`SidebarStore`, `WindowStore`, `SettingsStore`, `Persistence`, `RoomStore` with the seed; executable `room-persistence-harness`); `scripts/persistence-kill-test.sh`; `RoomWindowController` reports sidebar width/visibility (toggle, splitter drag end), frame and close; `AppDelegate` opens the store (`-RoomStateDirectory` override; DEBUG snapshot/spike runs use a temporary directory), restores the window, flushes on window close and writes the clean-exit flag at quit (`applicationWillTerminate`, and after `engine.run()` returns because CEF's terminate path skips it). Tests: Model 42, Store 21, UIKitCore +2; Room scheme and the no-engine CI spec build and pass (160 tests). App checks with a scratch state directory: first launch writes profile "Default", space "Space", a 240 pt window record and `running=1`; Quit sets `running=0`; a database edited to a 1111×777 frame at (150,120) with a hidden 333 pt sidebar reopens at exactly that frame (window-server bounds) and keeps both values. CEF smoke: quit 0.20 s, exit 0, no helpers left, no crash reports (load 14.8).
  - Deviations / decisions: (1) The 300 ms "debounce" starts at the first mutation and is not reset by later ones, so continuous mutation still writes ~3×/s (a resetting debounce would never write while the user drags). (2) Timestamps are INTEGER milliseconds (`Timestamp`), not `Date`: `Date` does not round-trip exactly through SQLite, which would break the writer's diff and the kill test's hashes. (3) `entry.isCollapsed` added for folders (not in the ROADMAP column list); column names are the Swift property names (`spaceID`, not `spaceId`). (4) Foreign keys are deferred and never cascade: the writer applies a whole-state diff in any order and the store removes dependents itself; `entry.faviconID` has no foreign key (cache semantics, a missing row = letter tile). (5) Interpretations: folder depth counts folder levels from the pinned root (top-level folder = 1, max 5); ⌘1–9 order skips children of collapsed folders and the pinned section when it is collapsed, and ⌘9 is the last item; the ⌘W fallback is the most recently used other entry of the space, then the first Today tab, then a new Today tab + palette (permanent) or the empty state (Today). (6) Deleting a space deletes its pinned and Today entries (essentials stay with the profile); spec §B moves them to the Archive, which is Chapter 4.1. (7) The window frame persists too, not only sidebar width/visibility. (8) "Exactly the last committed generation" is checked as: generation on disk ≥ last acknowledged commit, ≤ last begun, and the reopened content hashes to what was begun for that generation; no round ever reopened at an unacknowledged commit. (9) The kill harness is not in CI (one run takes ~60 s); it is `scripts/persistence-kill-test.sh`. (10) UI check in the real app (computer-use; the first access request had timed out unseen and was granted on retry): dragging the splitter from 240 to ~320 pt, then below the snap threshold, saved `sidebarWidth=319, sidebarVisible=0` (the pre-drag width kept, generation 3); Quit set `running=0`; the relaunch opened with the sidebar closed. View ▸ Hide Sidebar is disabled while Room is in the background (its target is reached through the key window), so the menu path is covered by `WindowStateCallbackTests` only.
  - Next: Phase 2.2a (sidebar tree, rows, clicks, menus, toasts), when the user says go.

  - Deviations / decisions: (1) The header row holds the traffic lights and the address field; the space name moved into the tree as the space-title row (spec §B) with the pinned-collapse chevron. Without a header view `SidebarView` still shows the title beside the lights (Phase 1.3 look and tests unchanged). Tests: Store +4 (group move, cap, nested folders, duplicates), Sidebar +11 (drop targets, grid position, multi-select, ⌘/⇧ clicks, balanced rows, controller drops: group conversion, drop-onto folder, ⌥ copy, full grid, outside URLs); Room scheme 195 tests pass locally with the window tier; the no-engine CI spec builds and passes with window suites skipped. CEF smoke: quit 0.21 s, no helpers left, no crash reports (load 13).
  - Bugs found by the captures and the profile, fixed: (1) the outline had width+height autoresizing inside the clip view, so a shorter window shrank it below its rows and the last rows could not be scrolled to until the next reload; height now comes from `tile()` in the body's layout (a headless test did not reproduce it, so none was kept). (2) The keyboard focus ring showed in inactive windows. (3) The trailing audio indicator's layer stayed behind while its row slid out in Clear; it is hidden for the slide. (4) Scroll cost per new row: row views were never reused, every cell rebuilt a tracking area each display cycle, letter tiles re-rendered glyphs per draw; fixing those took the 9000 pt/s step median from 8.06 to 4.87 ms. (5) `scripts/sidebar-snapshots.sh` left an `activate`-race relaunch of Room running without arguments on the real state directory (found holding the CEF profile lock); the script now stops such strays.
  - Deviations / decisions: (1) Enter on a focused row selects it; on the selected row it renames (reconciles §I.13 "Enter selects" with §I.9 / 2.2a "Enter renames"). (2) The folder preview opens only on collapsed folders (Arc's behaviour; an expanded folder shows its tabs already). (3) Folders take no part in multi-selection; they drag alone. (4) A drop from outside makes an entry of the target section's kind at the drop position (spec: "new Today tab at the drop position"; a Today tab elsewhere would not be at the drop position); the first one opens. (5) ⌥-drag of a folder is refused (copies of folders are undefined). (6) The full grid shows its badge while hovered and shakes on release; the drop returns false so the image slides back. The empty-grid slot appears for drags started in the sidebar only. (7) Space on a focused row (Peek) waits for 2.7 and is swallowed. (8) The 500 ms hold relies on AppKit's periodic drag updates to `NSOutlineView`; if they do not arrive without pointer movement, the first small move after 500 ms triggers it (in the list below). (9) ⌘D / ⇧⌘D / ⇧⌘K live in a Tab menu until 2.8 builds the full menu set. (10) The mid-drag capture is drawn by a DEBUG hook (line and lifted image inside the outline): a real drag's image is its own window and sessions do not drive the UI. (11) The scroll criterion is measured by proxy (60 Hz display here; `spike-results.md`). (12) Tasks 5 and 6 are one commit: the controller wiring, the focus extension and the drag extension depend on each other.
  - For the user to try (needs clicks, drags, keys): run `build/DerivedData/Build/Products/Debug/Room.app/Contents/MacOS/Room -RoomSidebarFixture demo`; drag a pinned row up and down (line moves with a haptic tick on a Force Touch trackpad, the row lifts, others animate on drop); drag a Today tab into the pinned section and back, and onto the Essentials grid; add essentials until 12 and drag another (badge, shake); hold a row over the middle of "Reading" for half a second without moving (drops into it), and over a pinned entry (new folder, renamed inline); ⌥-drag a tab (copy); drag a link from Safari and some text from TextEdit onto the sidebar; ⌘-click two Today tabs, ⇧-click a third, drag the group, right-click it (group menu), press Delete; ⌘D, ⇧⌘D, ⇧⌘K on the selected tab; click a row then ↑/↓/→/←/Enter/Esc; scroll the selected row out of view and click the accent line; double-click below the rows; rest the pointer on "Archive" (folder preview: type, ↑/↓, Enter); and on the laptop: `scripts/sidebar-snapshots.sh 500` plus a trackpad fling on the 500 fixture for the 120 Hz check.
  - For 2.3 (`SidebarHost`, the sidebar's expectations): the sidebar only writes the stores and calls the host for pages. `activate(entry)` is called after the store already selected and `markActive`d it (and for the first entry of a drop from outside); it must show the page, creating it from `entry.currentURL` if unloaded. `deactivate()` = empty card (2.3 puts the "Press ⌘T…" content there). `unload(id)` is called for Clear, Close Tabs Below/Other, group close, middle-click and close steps; it must be idempotent and drop the page without touching the store. `reset(entry)` comes after the store set `liveURL = storedURL`; `separate(id, into:)` moves the page to a new Today entry (keep the WebContents, re-key it). The host reports page changes with `pageStateDidChange(ids)` (loaded, loading, audible, muted, favicon) and writes title/URL through `SidebarStore.setLivePage`; `EntryPageState` is read synchronously per visible row and per drag image, so keep it cheap. Moves and conversions (drag, ⌘D, ⇧⌘D) never call the host: a moved entry keeps its page under the same id, whatever its kind. ⌘W should call `SidebarController.closeCommandTargets()` (the multi-selection, else the selected entry, per `permanentCloseBehavior`). `newTab()` and `editAddress()` are the palette's (2.4).

- 2026-10-03 — Phase 2.3 (Claude Code, Mac mini). Phase done.
  - Done: `Packages/Tabs` (new; in the Room scheme and the CI spec): `TabController` (`SidebarHost` on the `Engine` protocols: pages per entry, `PageDisplay` for the card, waits for the profile at launch and loads only the selection, `loadInBackground`, page observation → `setLivePage` / `setFavicon` / `pageStateDidChange`, every page's UI + navigation delegate with presentation forwarded to `pageUIDelegate`, new-tab dispositions → Today tabs per `NavigationRules.relatedTabIndex`, `window.close()` closes the entry, beforeunload "Stay" during ⌘W keeps the page), `DiscardPolicy` + lifecycle (idle sweep every 60 s, `DispatchSource` memory pressure), `TabSwitcher` (local key monitor, `TabSwitcherState`, glass list). Model: `NavigationRules.adjacent`, `relatedTabIndex`, favicon queries on `RoomDatabase`. Store: `ClosedTab`/`ClosedTabStack`/`SidebarStore.reopen`, `FaviconStore` (`RoomStore.favicons`). Sidebar: `close` public and pushing what it closes (also Clear and Close Tabs Below/Other), reset-only close navigates the kept page, `SidebarTabCommands` (⇧⌘T, ⌘1–9, adjacent, `recentEntries`, ⇧⌘M), `FaviconView` public. Engine: `EngineApplication.isTerminating` (CEF, FakeEngine, NoEngine; `docs/engine-api.md` row), `FakeWebContents.simulateFavicon`. Card: `CardEmptyStateView`. App: TabController in place of `SidebarPages` (deleted); `DebugSession` → `PageUIDelegate` without its page list; debug panel keeps URL field, back/forward/reload, DevTools, overlays and builds its view only while open; ⌘T/⌘L prompt in `URLPrompt`; menus Close Tab ⌘W, Reopen Closed Tab ⇧⌘T, Select Next/Previous Tab ⇧⌘]/⇧⌘[, Next/Previous Tab in Sidebar ⌥⌘↓/⌥⌘↑, Tab 1–8 / Last Tab ⌘1–⌘9, Mute Tab ⇧⌘M; Debug ▸ Simulate Memory Pressure / Discard Background Pages (results in the log). DEBUG: `SpikeSession` (Phase 1.4 spikes on Today tabs), spikes `tabs`, `switch`, `relaunch`, `capture` (`TabSpikes.swift`), fixture `-RoomSidebarFixture 50`, `scripts/tabs-measure.sh`; an explicit `-RoomStateDirectory` now wins over the DEBUG temporary directory. Tests: Tabs 23 (pages per entry, launch loads only the selection, empty state, page state into the store, favicon cache, `window.close()`, ⌘W on Today/last Today/pinned, ⇧⌘T for Today, pinned and after Clear, beforeunload Stay, related tabs foreground/background chain/pinned opener, idle and pressure discard, discarded reload, switcher state, recent list, ⌘1–9 and adjacent), Model +2, Store +1; Room scheme 221 tests pass with the window tier; the no-engine CI spec builds and passes with window suites skipped. CEF smoke: quit 0.17 s, no helpers left, no crash reports (load 3.4). Numbers in `spike-results.md` (Phase 2.3); captures in `research/screenshots/phase-2.3/`.
  - Bugs found on CEF and in captures, fixed: (1) quitting removed every Today tab: CEF closes all browsers at quit and the controller took each close for `window.close()` (and activated the next entry, which loaded a new page and kept the quit waiting); `EngineApplication.isTerminating` lets it leave entries alone. (2) Menu items with a lowercase key equivalent and a Shift mask (⇧⌘T, ⇧⌘W, ⇧⌘Z, and 2.2's ⇧⌘D and ⇧⌘K) never match: AppKit compares the event's characters ("T"), so shifted shortcuts now name the shifted character ("T", "}"). (3) The hidden debug panel's SwiftUI view re-rendered on every switch (~3 ms, Time Profiler). (4) The ⌃Tab switcher's highlight was placed before the panel had a size and never showed. (5) `scripts/run-spike.sh` kept activating Room after the spike quit, which relaunched it without arguments on the real state directory (it hung the first measurement); it now follows its own instance, stops activating at the end and quits strays.
  - Deviations / decisions: (1) `EngineApplication.isTerminating` added to the protocol (Chromium `browser_shutdown::IsTryingToQuit()`). (2) A tab opened from a permanent entry goes to the top of Today (§A.7 defines only Today openers; same place as 2.2's Duplicate); further links from one opener follow the previous one until the user switches tabs (Firefox `insertRelatedAfterCurrent`). (3) ⇧⌘T reopens Today tabs (one ⌘W, or a whole Clear / Close Tabs Below / Close Other Tabs at once, where they were) and a permanent entry's page at the URL it had before ⌘W's reset; Remove has its own Undo and is not on the stack; 25 items, per window, not kept across launches. (4) Memory pressure discards the older half of the eligible pages on a warning and all of them when critical (spec: LRU first). (5) Discard is `WebContents.discard()` (CEF: renderer closed, URL/title kept, back/forward lost, B-13); a discarded row shows as unloaded (dimmed, no changed dot). Pages with unsaved forms or in PiP are not exempt on CEF (B-17). (6) ⌘T tabs still go to the end of Today (2.2's placement; the palette decides in 2.4). (7) Middle-click Unload on the selected entry leaves it selected with the empty state in the card (2.2's behaviour). (8) The ⌃Tab list shows after ⌃ is held 120 ms; a quicker tap switches straight to the previous tab; W closes the highlighted entry through the sidebar's close (a permanent entry follows the ⌘W preference). (9) Favicons are written synchronously on the main thread (one small PNG per host and icon change, unchanged icons skipped by SHA-256). (10) The Debug menu's FakeEngine items (Navigate Selected Page, Toggle Audio) are gone; FakeEngine pages remain only for the DEBUG sidebar captures. (11) The CEF checks ran as key events sent through `NSApp.sendEvent` (page focused for the reserved keys) instead of by hand; the by-hand pass is below. Tabs: `openBackgroundTab(url:)`, `openLocation()` (⌘L) beside `editAddress()` (header click, docked), `onEditAddress(docked:)`. App: ⌘T / ⌘L / the header field / "New Tab" open the palette; the overlay is prewarmed after launch; `URLPrompt` and the Debug ▸ Show Debug Toolbar panel (`DebugToolbar`) deleted; DEBUG spikes `palette` and `palettemeasure` (`PaletteSpikes.swift`), `timedTurn` shared with 2.3's switch timing. Design: `PaletteMetrics`, `Typography.paletteField`, `Timing.typedTextMemory`, `Symbol.openURL` (`docs/design-tokens.md`). Tests: Omnibox 25 (fixup and classification, verbatim, both entry providers, bands, groups, dedupe, the 8-row cap with the kept search row, inline rules, ⇥ cycling, reused keys never stale), Palette 12 (the §C table, unchanged ⌘L, Esc, the 45 s memory; on FakeEngine: ⌘T Enter/⌘Enter/⌥Enter, ⌘L reload and navigate, Esc then close, typed text kept and expired, switching to a tab in another space, a tab switch dismissing), Sidebar −2 (the fixup tests moved); Room scheme 256 tests pass with the window tier; the no-engine CI spec builds and passes with window suites skipped. CEF: `scripts/run-spike.sh palette -RoomSidebarFixture demo` passes every check before and after the deletion (a URL opened and edited through the palette alone); smoke quit 0.18 s, no helpers left, no crash reports (load 5.4). Numbers and checks in `spike-results.md` (Phase 2.4a); captures in `research/screenshots/phase-2.4a/`.
  - Numbers (Debug build, 500-entry fixture, session locked so the window was occluded): open 4.9–6.6 ms in our code, 15.3–16.3 ms median / 19.6–20.1 ms p95 for the whole run-loop turn including AppKit's layout and commit; no display-link frame to time first frame against while occluded. Keystroke to rows: 3.6–4.0 ms median / 6.4–6.6 ms p95 for every keystroke after a query's first; the first keystroke (the list appears, the panel grows) 22 ms median. Providers + scorer alone 2.4–2.5 ms median / 6.1 ms p95.
  - Bugs found by the spike and the captures, fixed: (1) ⌘⏎ never committed: the field editor has no command for it and a key equivalent only reaches the panel in the key window (a local key monitor now handles it). (2) In dark mode the reused overlay drew row titles in light-mode black (SwiftUI `.primary` kept a stale appearance); rows use dynamic `NSColor`s. (3) Building the sidebar snapshot cost 6 ms of every open at 500 entries, and matching re-parsed every entry's URLs on every keystroke; both are cached now (query 3.8 → 2.4 ms median).
  - Deviations / decisions: (1) `URLFixup` lives in `Omnibox`, and Sidebar depends on Omnibox for it (drops keep their helper as an extension in Sidebar): the verbatim provider needs the same fixup, and Omnibox must not depend on a UI package. (2) "Open tabs" are Today entries in every space; "Pinned & essentials" are permanent entries (essentials of the active space's profile only); the entry in the card is never suggested. (3) An entry whose URL is exactly what was typed is the top hit and Enter switches to it (spec "exact tab match"), deduped against the verbatim URL; ⌘Enter still opens a copy. (4) Rows: the top hit, then the up to 7 most relevant other matches, shown in the spec's group order (relevance picks which rows, the group decides where); groups have no headers, a 4 pt gap separates them; the verbatim alternative (the search for a sure URL, the URL for "node.js") sits in the search group and the search row is never cut. (5) A bare "name.tld" is a sure URL for well-known generic TLDs and ISO country codes, otherwise the search is the top hit with the URL as the alternative; a leading "?" forces a search. (6) ⌘L with the URL untouched shows no rows (Enter reloads); empty ⌘T shows no rows (Tab-from-empty commands are 2.4b). (7) Esc in ⌘L with edited text restores the URL first and closes on the second Esc ("Esc restores"). Typed text is kept for 45 s when the palette is dismissed another way (click outside, ⌘T/⌘L again, a tab switch) and forgotten on Esc or a commit; ⌘T's text is never kept (there is no tab). (8) Docked (header click): the panel's left edge on the field, centred on it, 640 pt wide; with the sidebar hidden ⌘L and the header path fall back to centred. (9) A click outside dismisses and is not passed to the page or the sidebar. (10) The pointer moves the highlight only when it actually moved, so rows changing under a still pointer never take Enter's target. (11) ⇥ / ⇧⇥ cycle groups already (they are field-editor commands, task 1). (12) New tabs still go to the end of Today; the spec names no position. (13) Inline host completion: the field, the scorer and the deletion rule are in place, but no provider supplies completions until 2.4b's history (spec: "from history only"). (14) Paste & Go (§C top hit) is not done: reading the pasteboard on every open can raise macOS 26's paste-access prompt; 2.4b should detect a URL without reading it and read only when the row is chosen. (15) The Debug panel's other controls go with it: DevTools stays on View ▸ Developer Tools (⌥⌘I), back/forward/reload on the View menu, the status text returns as 2.6's status pill, `OverlaySpike` stays for `SpikeRunner`. (16) The search engine is Google in the palette's context until the Settings choice (2.8). (17) The session was locked all along (B-18): captures are in-process renders like 2.3's (glass as a flat stand-in), the window was never key, and the Phase 1.4 `keys` spike recorded ⌘C/⌘V not reaching the page ("page saw []"), consistent with Chromium focusing its view only in the key window; ⌘T/⌘L still reached the app in all three layouts. (18) Commits: the Palette package (overlay, commands, semantics) is one commit because the controller and the semantics it executes were written and tested together; the app wiring is a separate commit.
  - For the user to try (needs keys and clicks, screen unlocked): `scripts/run-spike.sh palettemeasure -RoomSidebarFixture 500` once for the open-to-next-frame number on a visible window (and the keystroke numbers with CEF compositing); then run `build/DerivedData/Build/Products/Debug/Room.app/Contents/MacOS/Room -RoomSidebarFixture demo -RoomStateDirectory /tmp/room-2.4a` and: ⌘T (glass panel scales in over the card), type "ar" (top hit, open tab, pinned and essentials), ↑/↓, ⇥/⇧⇥, move the pointer over rows (highlight follows), click a row, ⌘-click a row (a copy opens); ⌘T "example.com" with Enter, ⌘Enter, ⌥Enter (new tab / current tab / background); ⌘L (URL selected), Enter (reload), type and Esc twice; type in ⌘L, click outside, ⌘L again within 45 s (the text is back) and after 45 s (the URL); click the header's address field (docked) and press ⌘S then ⌘L (centred); ⌘T then ⌘T, ⌘L then ⌘T (closes); after closing, type into a page's text field (focus returned to the page); type in the palette with a Lithuanian layout and an IME; switch light/dark with the palette open; ⌘C/⌘V in a page text field (the `keys` spike could not see it while locked).
  - For 2.4b (where history, commands and suggest plug in): a provider is an `OmniboxProvider` (synchronous, `matches(for:in:)`); add it to `Omnibox.localProviders` or pass providers to `Omnibox(providers:)` (`PaletteController.omnibox` is settable). History: score in `Relevance.history` (600…1299), and when the top match completes the input inline (`InlineCompletion.suffix(completing:with:)`) give it `Relevance.inlineHistory` (1410…1449) and set `inlineCompletion`; `OmniboxResult` already keeps inline text only for a history top hit and drops it after a deletion, and `PaletteController.applyInlineCompletion` selects it in the field; mark history rows `isDeletable` and handle ⇧Delete / ⌘⌥Delete in `PaletteController.control(_:textView:doCommandBy:)` (and the hover ×). The context is a snapshot built at open; the history provider should hold its own database reader and run FTS per keystroke. Commands: `MatchAction.command(id:)`, `MatchIcon.symbol`, `Relevance.commands`, group `.commands`; `PaletteSemantics` maps them to `CommitAction.runCommand(id:)`, which `PaletteController.perform` ignores today; `>` and Tab-from-empty go in `query`/`cycleGroup` (Tab with an empty field). Site search: a keyword chip mode in the palette, results as `.search` actions with the site's URL. Suggest (asynchronous): rank the synchronous matches plus whatever has arrived with `OmniboxResult(ranking:input:)` again and keep the highlighted match by id; `ProviderKind.suggest`, `Relevance.suggest` (500…899) and the search group exist; the off switch must stop requests before they are made. Measuring: `palettemeasure` splits first and later keystrokes and reports providers + scorer alone (`onQueryTimed`); the 50k-row run should add history rows to the 500 fixture. `didCommitNavigation` (task 4) is still to add to the Engine protocol; `TabController.track` is where URL changes arrive today.
  - Visible-window rerun (the user, same day, screen unlocked, `window_main: true`): open to next frame **25.8 ms median / 36.8 ms p95** (open turn 20.3 / 22.7 ms, our code 7.3 / 8.8 ms); later keystrokes 4.5 / 11.1 ms, first keystroke of a query 18.5 / 27.2 ms; providers + scorer 2.5 / 5.3 ms (`spike-results.md`). The palette shows 1.5–2 frames at 60 Hz after ⌘T; for 2.4b: build the context on the first keystroke rather than at open (the empty palette needs no snapshot), then re-measure.

- 2026-10-03 — Phase 2.4, session **2.4b** (Claude Code, Mac mini). The 2.4a carry-overs, the rest of task 3, tasks 4 and 6, the exit criteria; Phase 2.4 marked done.
  - Done: `Model` schema v2 (`history_url.host`, `history_url.frecency`, `history_host` aggregate with the summed frecency and best URL per host; v1 rows backfilled), `History` (record a visit off the main thread, titles after the visit, reloads and `about:`/`data:` not recorded, fragments folded, trigram search with a 2,000-row frecent scan before the index, host-prefix rows for one- and two-letter text, `hosts(prefix:)`, delete by URL and by host, bulk import, most frecent for ⌘Y), `Frecency` (visit weights typed 2, bookmark 1.5, link/other 1, search/keyword/form 0.3, half-life 30 days, stored as a logarithm referenced to a fixed epoch so order never needs recomputing), `Settings.searchSuggestions` (on) and `searchEngine` (`google`). `Engine`: `NavigationDelegate.webContents(_:didCommitNavigation:transition:)` with a default no-op; FakeEngine reports every commit; CEF reports `OnLoadStart` one turn later (dropped for error pages), substitutes the transition its last `load` asked for (CEF commits every browser load as TT_EXPLICIT; a discarded page's revival is `.reload`), and reports same-document address changes as `.link`; `docs/engine-api.md` row. `Tabs`: visits recorded per committed navigation of an entry's page; the palette's URLs open with their transition (typed, generated, keyword); restored and unloaded pages come back as `.reload`, unloaded pinned entries as `.bookmark`; `favicon(forURL:)` for history rows. `Omnibox`: `HistoryProvider` (match quality + frecency in the history band; inline completion of a host or a path when typed before or visited 3 times; none after a deletion), the scorer's tab match (history naming an open entry switches to it), `CommandRegistry` (the spec §C list A–Z plus Focus on / Move to {space}) and `CommandsProvider` (`>` mode lists all A–Z or matches words and keywords; outside `>` only 3+ letters starting a word, always last), `SiteSearch` (yt, wiki, gh, maps, ddg) with the hint row and the chip mode (site search + that site's history), `SuggestClient` (URLSession without cookies, cache or credentials; 120 ms debounce; each keystroke cancels the request in flight; the switch checked before the debounce, after it and on arrival; URLs never sent; 5 suggestions; OpenSearch and DuckDuckGo formats), `OmniboxInput` modes. `Palette`: the sidebar snapshot built on the first keystroke; history rows with site favicons; ⇧Delete / ⌘⌥Delete and the hover × remove history; ⇥ from empty enters `>`, a keyword + ⇥ the chip, Backspace on empty leaves it; suggestions merged as they arrive with the highlighted row kept by id, older ones kept while they still fit; Paste & Go on an empty ⌘T (`NSPasteboard.detectedPatterns`, read only on commit); ⌘Y opens on recent history. `Sidebar`: palette commands on the selection (copy URL, duplicate, unload, reset, new folder, move to space). App: the commands table, a Settings window (Search: engine, "Show search suggestions"; SwiftUI leaf, ⌘,), ⌘Y, the switch observed so turning it off drops shown suggestions at once. DEBUG: `HistoryFixture` (`-RoomHistoryRows N`), spikes `palette2` and `suggestcheck`, `palettemeasure` with phases and a running display link, `-RoomSuggestProxy`, `-RoomSuggestionsOff`. Design: `Timing.suggestDebounce`, chip and × metrics, `Symbol.appearance` (`docs/design-tokens.md`). Tests: Model 59 (+17: frecency, recording, search, hosts, migration), Engine 54 (+1 commits), Tabs 25 (+2 recording), Omnibox 44 (+19: history provider, inline rules, tab match, cap, commands, site search, suggest client against a fake transport and a hand-released debounce), Palette 21 (+9: lazy snapshot, commands mode, chip, history + delete, transitions, suggestions and the switch, Paste & Go, ⌘Y). Room scheme 301 tests with the window tier; the CI spec passes with window suites skipped.
  - Numbers (`spike-results.md`, Phase 2.4b; screen sharing on, 60 Hz display, load 5–21): with 50k history rows local results p95 9.85–9.93 ms Debug / 5.93 ms optimised; whole keystroke turn, later keys p95 13.65–14.22 ms Debug / 12.13 ms optimised; a query's first keystroke 22.2–24.7 ms median. Open: our code 2.3 ms, turn 2.9 ms, next frame 6.2 ms median / 15.0 ms p95; the 2.4a commit measured the same way on the same machine 17.4–17.7 ms. Suggestions off: 0 proxy requests and 0 CEF net-log request events while typing; on: one request per pause.
  - Bugs found by measuring, profiling and running on CEF, fixed: (1) AppKit's password AutoFill walked every view in the window and built a one-time-code window on each palette focus (6–7 ms per open at 500 rows): the field declares `contentType = .URL` and has no key-view neighbours. (2) Adding and removing the overlay moved the rows' hosting view into the window each open: it stays, hidden (and its `hitTest` returns nil when hidden). (3) SwiftUI rebuilt every row view per keystroke (identity by match id) and re-measured rows whose height changed: fixed position slots at full height, never emptied. (4) The scorer normalised URLs with `URLComponents` on every dedupe comparison: computed once. (5) With inline completion prevented (after a deletion) the host match kept the inline band and stayed the top hit. (6) CEF reports `pushState` as loading, so same-document commits were never reported; then their title was written before the visit existed. (7) Inline completion looked at the most frecent host only, so a link-only host blocked a typed one.
  - Deviations / decisions: (1) Schema v2 instead of v1's history tables alone (host key, frecency, `history_host`); the frecency is an exponential decay stored as a logarithm, no daily job. (2) `didCommitNavigation` has a default empty implementation; `NoEngineApplication` makes no web contents, so it has nothing to implement. (3) Restored and unloaded Today pages load as `.reload` (not visits); before, `.other`. (4) Error pages and reloads are not recorded; `#fragments` fold into their page. (5) Deleting an inline host suggestion deletes the host's history (one URL would bring it straight back). (6) Commands of later phases (screenshot, archive, find, new space, split, private window, print, routing, next/previous space) are hidden in the palette until their phase, not shown disabled; Focus on / Move to {space} appear when there are other spaces; the appearance commands are not persisted (Settings v0, 2.8). (7) Site search enters on ⇥ only, not Space ("maps of europe" stays a query); keywords are defaults in code until Settings ▸ Search (4.4); the chip mode shows the site search and that site's history, no suggestions. (8) A Settings window with only the Search section now (the off switch must live in Settings; 2.8 builds Settings v0 around it). (9) Suggestions: typed text that is surely a URL is never sent; the session has no cookies, so Kagi's autosuggest (which wants the user's session) likely answers nothing (not tested). (10) Paste & Go shows on an empty ⌘T only, titled "Paste and Go — Link on the clipboard" (the URL is not read until chosen). (11) History naming an open entry switches to it (Chromium's tab match). (12) ⌘Y opens the palette on the most frecent history (shortcuts.md: "palette history group until Chapter 3"). (13) The next-frame probe changed (one running display link; the 2.4a probe's new link added its start-up latency); the 2.4a code was rebuilt with the new probe for the comparison. (14) A query's first keystroke stays above one frame with SwiftUI rows (12 ms of SwiftUI text for eight new rows plus the glass growing); PLAN §4 says palette rows are SwiftUI, so it stays and the question goes to the user. (15) Captures with suggestions off: deterministic and no live third-party text (a first run had Google's suggestions for "gi", including a person's name). (16) The docked geometry has no recorded decision: 2.4a's kept, asked in the report. (17) Commits: the palette integration (modes, suggest merge, deletion, Paste & Go) is one commit because it is one controller change; the performance work is one commit after profiling.
  - For the user to try (needs keys and clicks; best without screen sharing): `scripts/run-spike.sh palettemeasure -RoomSidebarFixture 500 -RoomHistoryRows 50000` once on the laptop's 120 Hz panel (B-20). Then run `build/DerivedData/Build/Products/Debug/Room.app/Contents/MacOS/Room -RoomSidebarFixture demo -RoomStateDirectory /tmp/room-2.4b` and: visit a few sites through ⌘T, then ⌘T and type the start of one (inline completion selected; →/End accepts, Backspace removes it and it does not come back on that text); ⇧Delete and ⌘⌥Delete on a history row, and its × on hover; ⌘T ⇥ (commands), `>re`, Enter on Reload; `yt` ⇥ `lofi` Enter (YouTube results), Backspace on the empty chip; type a query and pause (suggestions join the search group, the highlighted row stays put); with suggestions showing, open Room ▸ Settings… (⌘,) and turn "Show search suggestions" off (the rows go at once), then type again (none appear); copy a URL in another app, ⌘T (Paste and Go; no paste prompt until Enter), Enter; ⌘Y (recent history); a Lithuanian layout and an IME in the field; light/dark with the chip shown.
  - For 2.5 (spaces): `OmniboxEntry.spaceName` drives the chip on rows from other spaces and `CommandRegistry.focus(space:name:)` / `move(toSpace:name:)` already produce one command per other space (`AppDelegate.paletteCommands`); add New Space, Next/Previous Space there when they exist (the registry has them). A space switcher in the palette can be the `>` list filtered by "focus", or its own provider in the commands band. Palette commands run on `SidebarController`'s selection; space-aware ones should take the window's active space. History is one table for the whole database, not per profile: when spaces get separate profiles (2.5 task 4), decide whether history follows the profile (a `profileID` column in a v3 migration) — Chromium keeps history per profile. Essentials are filtered to the active space's profile in `PaletteController.makeContext`. The snapshot is rebuilt per open from `store.entries`; a space switch while the palette is open dismisses it (selection change).

Cookies: Default sees `home`, Work sees `work`, a Default space sees `home`, a tab moved to Work loads again and sees `work`.
  - Bugs found by running it, fixed: (1) a new list's outline asked for rows the moment its data source was set, before it was registered, and got the active list's count (crash in `outlineView(_:child:ofItem:)`). (2) A crash left macOS's "reopen windows?" alert blocking the next launch; spike runs pass `-ApplePersistenceIgnoreState YES`. (3) Hidden outlines make no row views: the first switch to each space made them during the slide (~65 ms); lists are drawn once off screen now. (4) Each switch re-laid out the footer and set the hidden window title synchronously (≈4 ms together). (5) The hover name truncated against "+".
  - §B checklist: space data (id, name, icon, theme colours/algorithm/opacity, profile, position) ✓; essentials per profile, crossfade when the profile changes ✓; creation by "+" ▸ New Space, swipe past the last space, palette New Space ✓; form fields, placeholder, "Click to add a color", algorithms, profile tooltip, Enter/Esc ✓ (deviations 1–2); switching by icon, ⌃1…⌃9/⌃0, ⌥⌘←/→ with wrap, swipe (25% + velocity, rubber band, edge form), ⌃+wheel, Focus on {space} ✓; slide 250 ms + tint crossfade + sliding indicator, Reduce Motion crossfade ✓; active 1.15× and the name while hovering ✓; title row with chevron, click-to-rename, "…" ✓; the space menu ✓ (routing settings disabled for 4.2); Delete Space dialog ✓ (deviation 3); profiles as cookie jars, move = reload with the one-time dialog ✓; routing — Chapter 4.2.
  - Deviations / decisions: (1) The algorithm is a pop-up menu, not a segmented control: five labels do not fit a 240–280 pt sidebar. (2) The form takes the list's place in the sidebar (it slides in like a space, as a swipe past the last space reveals it) rather than a sheet; Change Icon… and Edit Theme… open the same form focused on that part. The form has no opacity or texture control (B-23). (3) Delete Space asks "Delete “{name}”?" with the entry count, without "This cannot be undone": an Undo toast (8 s) brings it back until the Archive (B-15, 4.1); its pages unload. (4) Reorder Spaces shows Zen's hint ("Drag the space icons at the bottom of the sidebar to reorder them"); icons are always draggable (>5 pt), there is no separate mode. (5) The algorithms fill the wells the user left empty at fixed hue offsets; a filled well always wins (ours; Zen links dots on a wheel). A space without colours keeps the neutral window. (6) Only the lists slide (space title, pinned, Today); the header, the essentials grid (crossfades on a profile change) and the footer stay. (7) Engine profiles are named after the model profile (`Room Profile <name>`, characters a path cannot hold replaced); names are unique; profiles cannot be renamed or deleted yet (B-22). (8) Unload Space keeps the page in the card. (9) Resting on a space icon during a drag switches to that space so the drop can land in its list; dropping on the icon moves Today tabs to the top of its Today and pinned entries/folders to the end of its pinned list; essentials never move between spaces; URLs from outside become Today tabs there. (10) ⌃digit goes to the page first (not reserved, `docs/shortcuts.md`) and may be taken by macOS's "Switch to Desktop N" shortcuts if those are on. (11) Prepared lists cost ~45 ms per space on idle turns after launch. (12) Captures come from the window server through the script (real pages); the window was never key, so its traffic lights are grey and `activityOverride` forces the active sidebar. (13) Commits: the sidebar's spaces work (panes, slide, footer, title row, menu, form, swipe) is one commit because the pane refactor carries all of it.

  - For 2.6 (chrome surfaces): palette rows move to AppKit first (task 0); the palette's context has `activeProfileID` and space chips. Every engine profile gets the same `downloadsDelegate` (`AppDelegate.startSession` and `requestEngineProfile`); `DownloadsDelegate.profile(_:…)` names the engine profile, and `TabController.engineProfiles` maps model profile → engine profile, so the downloads popover can say which profile (and, from the page that started it, which space) a download came from — one list for the window across profiles is the simple reading of spec §F. Popups and `window.open` pages are created by the engine in the opener's context; `TabController.adoptOpenedPage` puts them in the opener's space (so the same profile). Permissions and site data are per engine profile (CEF request context): the site panel and permission popover should act on the current page's profile (`Page.profileID`). The footer now holds the downloads button left of the space icons (`SidebarFooterView`); its progress ring goes there. The space form is the pattern for SwiftUI forms in the sidebar.

Task 2 — `Chrome` package: `PagePrompts` (one queue per page; the card shows the front dialog of the page it shows, `beforeunload` at once; every engine completion answered once: by the user, with the escape answer when the page closes, or never when the engine withdrew it), `PromptRules`, `PageChrome` (the task-2 members of `WebContentsUIDelegate`), `PagePromptPresenter` + AppKit views: the dialog sheet over a dimmed page (glass panel; Return/Esc; prompt field; auth with username/password and Tab), the certificate interstitial (Go Back default, Show Details, Visit … Anyway only when bypassable), the permission popover on the header's site button (Don't Allow · Allow This Time for camera/mic/location · Allow; closing = dismissed); `ContextMenu` (`MenuModel` → `NSMenu`, Room's Open Link in New Tab / Open Link in Peek (disabled until 2.7) / Copy Link and image items first, stray separators trimmed, the engine hears once); `FileChooser` (`accept` → content types, open/multiple/folder/save sheets). `Engine`: `CertificateRules` (overridable codes; without HSTS knowledge only IP addresses and local names). `EngineCEF`: `--disable-chrome-login-prompt`; `isOverridable` from the rule. `Tabs`: `openLink(_:from:)` (a background Today tab under the opener, §A.7), `entryID(for:)` public, page closes forwarded to the UI delegate.   - Numbers (`spike-results.md`, Phase 2.6a; 60 Hz, screen sharing on, load 4.5–7.7; before = the starting commit with only the new probes): a query's first keystroke 26.6 / 25.9 → **17.9 / 18.9 ms median** (p95 35.0 / 36.5 → 21.4 / 21.0); later keystrokes 7.96 / 7.77 → **5.13 / 4.90 ms** (p95 15.1 / 16.5 → 9.6 / 10.4; 2.4b recorded p95 13.7–14.2); with the first key on a performance core 17.85 → **7.53 ms** (query 4.80 → 2.27 ms). Open call 2.5–2.8 ms, unchanged. On CEF (`scripts/chrome-surfaces.sh`, light and dark): confirm true/false, prompt "Grace", alert acknowledged; `beforeunload` on ⌘W: Stay keeps the page (in a new Today entry, as 2.3's `stay`), Leave closes it; the auth sheet 310–335 ms after the 401, signed in; the interstitial (−202, overridable on 127.0.0.1), Go Back, then Visit reaches the page; location and notification popovers answered (notifications "denied" remembered); file choosers `public.image` / CSV+PDF multiple / folders; the link menu `Open Link in New Tab | Open Link in Peek | Copy Link | — | Copy`, the new tab lands below its page, the page stays selected; `window.open` popup → a selected Today tab with `window.opener` (message received), its `window.close()` closes the entry; `target=_blank` → a Today tab without opener. Smoke: quit 0.20 s, exit 0, no helpers left, no crash reports.
  - Bugs found by running it, fixed: (1) HTTP auth never reached `GetAuthCredentials`: under the Chrome bootstrap Chrome's own login prompt answered the 401 (the spike's typed credentials went into it) — `--disable-chrome-login-prompt`. (2) The main queue does not drain while an `NSMenu` tracks, so closing a menu from a dispatched block hung (the spike now closes it from a timer in the common modes; `ContextMenu` cancels after `popUp` returns, not on the main queue). (3) A `-l` window capture includes the window's child windows: the popover needs no screen-rect capture, which took in other apps' windows (those captures were deleted, never committed). (4) The bitmap letter tiles of the first task-0 commit were drawn at a quarter size (the context was in pixels); fixed before the captures. (5) Unknown MIME types in `accept` became dynamic UTTypes that no file matches.
  - Deviations / decisions: (1) **Task 0's target is not met by 2.4b's method**: 17.9–18.9 ms, not under 8.33 ms. The System Trace shows each first key, arriving after ≥ 100 ms idle, running ~18 ms on an efficiency core before the scheduler moves the main thread to a P core; on a P core the first key is 7.5 ms. A display link, typing outside the open animation and a `.latencyCritical` activity did not change it. The rows themselves were not the main cost (AppKit rows alone: 22.2 → 22.0 ms); PLAN §4's UI row amended with this. The 120 Hz laptop run is still B-20. (2) CEF cannot say whether a host has HSTS (`strict_enforcement` never reaches `OnCertificateError`) and its `Continue()` would bypass HSTS: Visit … Anyway only for local hosts and IP addresses on CEF (B-24). (3) Go Back on a tab with no history leaves Chrome's "Privacy error" page (the cancelled navigation commits it; it knows HSTS) — B-25 for the user. (4) Geolocation grants are not remembered on CEF ("prompt" after Allow; the request itself was granted); camera and microphone could not be exercised (no devices) — B-26. (5) JS dialogs and auth/certificate prompts wait for their page to be in the card; `beforeunload` shows at once over whatever the card shows (a page being closed has left its entry). Chrome marks a waiting background tab; Room records it (`onPageNeedsAttention`) but has no row mark yet. (6) The dialog panel is an AppKit glass panel near the card's top, not SwiftUI (keyboard handling with the CEF view next to it was simpler in AppKit). (7) Open Link in New Tab opens in the background below the page (as ⌘-click), not selected. (8) Copy Link writes plain text, without a toast. (9) The first two spike runs used the real engine directory, leaving test-origin state for 127.0.0.1:8766/8767 there (a certificate exception, notifications denied, a geolocation decision); the script now uses a throwaway `-RoomEngineDirectory`. (10) Commits: task 2 is one commit (the prompts, menus and file chooser share `PageChrome` and its presenter).

B-20 (palettemeasure on the 120 Hz laptop) now includes the first key.

Still without UI for the exit checklist: `didChangeStatusText` (status pill, task 3), `webContentsDidRequestEnterFullscreen`/`ExitFullscreen` (element fullscreen), `processGone` (a crashed-page view in the card: `PageContainerView.promptLayer` is the place), and the `DownloadsDelegate` members (task 1; `PageUIDelegate` still holds the 2.3 placeholder). Already handled earlier: keys (2.3), audible/muted and security state (sidebar and header, 2.2–2.3), `NavigationDelegate` (2.3, 2.4b); `didConsumeKey` is never called on CEF. The site panel should open from `AddressFieldView.siteButtonView` (the popover's anchor) and act on the page's profile; `scripts/chrome-surfaces.sh` and its server are the pattern for the 2.6b pages (downloads can be served from the same server).

Tests: Chrome 39 (+20: crash view, download names/folder/list/progress/badge, find counting and stepping, zoom steps, status pill timing and dodge, site panel mapping, rows, origin, model, clearing, defaults), Tabs 33 (+3: interstitial title, Go Back with and without history), Card 8 (+1: the divider's split); Room scheme 378 tests with the window tier, all passing; the no-engine CI spec builds and passes with window suites skipped (after clearing a stale `build/DerivedDataCI` whose old `EngineCEF` products made `canImport(EngineCEF)` true locally).
  - Numbers (`spike-results.md`, Phase 2.6b; 60 Hz, load 4.7–6.4): find keystroke → highlighted match on a 2,000-paragraph page **21.3 / 20.7 ms** median (p95 23.8 / 26.5), → final count 21.8 / 21.7 ms; DevTools ⌥⌘I → docked front-end loaded **327.9 / 334.9 ms** median (attached to the page 0.5 ms later); status pill configured 600 ms, measured **604.7 / 618.7 ms** from the status text (CEF's status text 2.1–2.4 ms after a hover); print panel after 285 ms. All in light / dark.
  - Bugs found by running it, fixed: (1) CEF's first `Find` for a text only counts (active match 0): nothing was highlighted and ⌘G moved from nothing; a new text is now sent again as find-next ("0/400" → "1/400"). (2) A navigation under a pending `alert()` never committed on Alloy (no tab-modal dialog manager); `CEFWebContents` now answers pending JavaScript dialogs with Cancel when Room navigates and reports `webContentsDidDismissJavaScriptDialogs`. (3) CEF calls `OnDismissPermissionPrompt` after answered prompts too; only unanswered ones are reported now (a queued second prompt would have been withdrawn). (4) Download updates carry an empty suggested name; `CEFProfile` keeps the one from `OnBeforeDownload`. (5) `GetContentSetting` answers with the effective value, so the site panel listed all twelve permissions as set; values equal to the default no longer count. (6) The downloads popover opened below the footer, outside the window (`maxY` in a flipped view). (7) After a page's element fullscreen the toolbar came back hidden (traffic lights at 9,9 instead of 19,19); `windowDidExitFullScreen` shows it again. (8) Spike-side: a stale 2.6a test server held port 8766 (its pages served, no `/download`), and the new route matched `/downloads.html`; the script now checks its own server answers `/download`. Two test files from a probe landed in ~/Downloads and were deleted; the spike downloads into a throwaway folder.
  - Deviations / decisions: (1) The fly-in's arc follows the spec (min(2 × distance, 1200) pt) but its top is held inside the window; without a click in the last 3 s it starts at the card's centre. (2) Room-started navigations dismiss a page's JavaScript dialogs on CEF (Chrome's behaviour), so the engine-withdrawal member is exercised by a navigation, as ROADMAP asked. (3) The site panel shows Location, Camera, Microphone and Notifications always and other permissions only when the site has its own setting; Ask is offered only for permissions a site asks for. (4) Clear site data clears cookies and storage of the origin (CEF clears the HTTP cache for all origins only, so the cache is left). (5) The zoom indicator is Room's (Chrome's bubble is Views); CEF zoom is Chrome's per-host zoom (B-27). (6) Print stays CEF's app-modal panel (B-29). (7) DevTools closes on a tab switch (as before); the pane's share is a `UserDefaults` view preference, not model state. (8) The downloads list is not persisted (B-28); the toast for a finished download shows only with the sidebar hidden (spec §E), the dock bounces when Room is in the background. (9) ⌘= has no ⇧⌘= / ⌘+ alias: ⇧⌘= is New Split (2.7). (10) The status pill's dodge follows `mouseMoved` through a local event monitor only while the pill shows. Element fullscreen sets `BrowserWindowView.isPageFullscreen`, which hides the sidebar: hide/reveal must leave it alone while a page is fullscreen. The context menu's "Open Link in Peek" is wired to `RoomMenuAction.openLinkInPeek` (disabled while `PageChrome.isPeekAvailable` is false); `DelegateRecorder` and `-RoomSpikeOnly` make it cheap to re-run the delegate checklist after 2.7 changes the card.
