Event isolation: 0 clicks, 0 presses, 0 moves reached the page under the sidebar overlay (5 + 5) and under the Peek dim (3 moves and the closing click); a click in the Peek card reached the Peek page.
  - Bugs found by running it, fixed: (1) a `SidebarReveal` tick at exactly the deadline did not move the phase on (`now - since >= dwell` is false by one ulp; caught by the test; a timer would have re-armed at the same instant). (2) The revealed list showed its bottom overflow line: while hidden the outline's visible rect is empty, and on reveal the glass had not sized its content view yet; the hidden sidebar now keeps the overlay's size, the container is sized by the overlay, and the lines are measured again on unhide. (3) Untinted glass let page text read through the rows; the glass's own `tintColor` rendered near white and hid the white selection pill; a grey wash inside the glass replaced both (also in the cluster's pill, where page text read through behind the buttons). (4) Dark mode: Peek's card kept the dark card fill over a white page (it now follows the page's root background) and Peek's header and the toolbar drew dark-mode text on a light page (they take the appearance that reads on the page's fill). (5) Spike-side: 2.6b's status-pill dodge check polled every 20 ms and missed the move (the page clears its status text a few ms after the posted move); it polls every 1 ms now and reads `leading → trailing`, x 4 → 731, as in 2.6b.
  - Deviations / decisions: (1) The overlay is the window's own `SidebarView` moved into the glass, not a second tree; a grey wash inside the glass keeps rows and the pill readable (tokens). (2) The overlay's header has two rows: Back/Forward/Reload beside the traffic lights, the address field below (spec §E puts the buttons "in the revealed overlay header"; at 240 pt they do not fit beside the field). (3) The top 5% of the card is a drag handle without taking the page's clicks there: a press is the page's until it moves 3 pt, then it is a window drag and the page gets a mouse-up (B-32). (4) The cluster fades together with the window's own buttons (alpha, then hidden so they take no clicks); their frames never change. (5) Peek dims the content area (the page card), not the sidebar; a Peek belongs to the entry it opened over (at most one): selecting another entry hides it and coming back shows it again (spec §A.3's "Peek child of the entry"); unloading the entry closes it. (6) A click on a link is told from a page's `window.location` by the link under the pointer or keyboard focus (CEF's status text): CEF reports both as `TT_LINK` with a gesture (Blink only marks `location.replace()` and load-time script navigations as client redirects); with no input time known, a script navigation stays in place (B-33). (7) "OAuth redirects" covers OAuth requests (`client_id` with `redirect_uri` or `response_type`, SAML, OpenID) and identity-provider sign-in paths (`/signin`, `/login`, `/oauth`, `/authorize`, `/sso`, …), so a pinned site's "Sign in with …" link never opens in Peek. (8) Peek scales up from the last click (within 2 s) rather than the link's rect, which CEF does not report. (9) Esc is page-first in Peek: it closes Peek when the page did not consume it (an Esc that closes a site's own dialog keeps Peek). (10) The sidebar's address field keeps showing the entry's page while Peek shows; Peek's header has its domain. Menu commands (reload, back/forward, find, zoom, print) act on the focused page, Peek's when it shows. (11) ⇧-click peeks from any page, Today tabs included (spec §I.6 "and ⇧-click"); inside Peek links navigate in Peek and new windows become Today tabs under the entry. (12) Settings ▸ Links switch added now (2.8's Settings v0 lists it; Peek's rule needed it); the toolbar setting persists in `Settings` and shows with the sidebar docked too (Arc). (13) Space on a focused permanent row peeks its stored URL over the selected entry's page (when nothing else is selected it selects the row). (14) A Peek page cannot take element fullscreen (B-35). (15) The Public Suffix List copy skips IDN rules (B-34). (16) Commits: one per task, then the capture fixes, the spike with its pages, the captures.

Peek covers `rootView.currentLayout.card` (the whole card); with a split decide whether it covers the group or the focused pane, and wire `PeekHeaderView.split` and `AppDelegate.splitPeek` (⇧⌘O, a stub that is disabled). `TabController.peeks` is keyed by entry, so a split group's panes each keep their own. The sidebar's address field follows the selected entry; with panes it should follow the focused pane (spec §D). `SidebarHost` gained `peek(_:)` and `reopenPeek(url:over:)` (defaults do nothing).

  - Done: carry-over — the responder chain with a CEF page focused does reach `RoomWindowController` (`RenderWidgetHostViewCocoa → … → PageContainerView → CardView → BrowserWindowView → RoomWindow → RoomWindowController`), but `RoomWindow` comes first and NSWindow implements `toggleSidebar:` itself (AppKit's sidebar action), validating it disabled: the item was disabled in every state (the 2.1 note blamed the key window). Both items now go through `AppDelegate` (`hideOrShowSidebar:`, `keepSidebarOpen:`) to the window's controller, whose action is `toggleSidebarVisibility:` (the RoomCore host menu too); `layoutmodes` `menu` records the chain and validates both enabled docked and hidden, ⌘S through the menu; a unit test that the window answers neither action. Task 3 — Model: `SplitGroup.members` (pane order; schema v4 with a backfill; old JSON snapshots decode), `SplitRules` (ratios, the group row's anchor, focus after a pane leaves, ⌃⇧N / ⌃⇧] / ⌃⇧[, ⌘W on a pane, drop insertion and layout, the grip's sizes). Store: create / add / remove / dissolve / layout / ratios / move / replace, ≤4, splits dissolving under two everywhere entries leave (remove, Clear, a move to Essentials, a space deleted), Undo rejoining. Card: `SplitGeometry` (frames, resizers, a drag that moves only its two panes with the 7% / 120 pt minimum, drop zones and previews). Sidebar: the group row (`SplitGroupCellView`, tree hiding the other panes' rows, the pill and peeking follow it), clicks per segment, ⌥-click, row-centre join (`DropRules.Target.join`), Split With ▸, Join N Tabs, the pane menu's layout / Remove from Split View / Separate All Tabs, New Split View in "+", the split row dragging its panes together, `SidebarDragTypes`, `onEntryDrag`; split commands (`SidebarSplit.swift`) and ⌘W on a pane. Tabs: `PageDisplay.showSplit` / `SplitPresentation`, every pane's page made and visible (never discarded), the card following the split, empty panes without a page, `splitShownPeek`. App: `SplitView` / `SplitPaneView` (own `PageContainerView` each, fill per page), resizers, pane header on hover (domain, grip, layout menu, ×), focus follows a press in a pane (`PageHost` monitor), drop overlay over the card during sidebar drags, the focused pane in element fullscreen (B-35), prompts / find / zoom / pill / DevTools / the palette on the focused pane, Peek ▸ Split, the Split menu (⇧⌘= and ⌃⇧=, ⌃⇧-, ⌥⌘G/V/H/U, ⌃⇧1…4, ⌃⇧] / ⌃⇧[), palette New Split View / Unsplit, an empty pane removed when its palette closes without a page. Spike `split` in `layoutmodes`. Tokens: "Added in Phase 2.7b" in `docs/design-tokens.md`. Tests: Model 86 (+10), Store 34 (+4), Card 14 (+6), Sidebar 38 (+6), Tabs 50 (+4), UIKitCore 39 (+1); Room scheme 432 tests with the window tier, all passing; the no-engine CI spec builds and passes.
  - Numbers (`spike-results.md`, Phase 2.7b; 60 Hz, load 5.0–5.6, light / dark): split created → both panes laid out **12.8 / 13.5 ms** median main thread, → next frame 21.2 / 22.0 ms (a pane whose page the split makes: 22.5 ms laid out, 330 ms loaded); resizer drag with two loaded pages **16.67 ms** median, p95 and max (91 / 89 frames, none dropped); press in the other pane → header field updated **2.68 / 2.35 ms** main thread, → next frame 7.0 / 6.5 ms. Event isolation with Peek over a three-pane split: 0 clicks, presses and moves reached any pane. 2.7a's checks unchanged on the new card; the 2.6b delegate checklist calls all 26 members again.
  - Bugs found by running it, fixed: (1) View ▸ Hide Sidebar (above). (2) ⌥⌘H matched the app menu's Hide Others first and hid the other apps instead of stacking the panes; Hide Others lost the key (B-36 for 2.8).
  - Deviations / decisions: (1) A split's panes keep their kinds and places; the group row stands at the first pinned pane (else the first pane) and the others' rows are hidden, so a Today tab can share a split with a pinned entry (spec §A.7 "tabs in a split with a permanent member"); the pane order lives in the group (schema v4) because members can sit in two sections. (2) Essentials are never panes: an essential joins as a Today copy of its page (Zen duplicates permanent tabs into splits; a grid tile cannot hold the group row). (3) Row-centre join applies to Today rows and to anything but a pinned entry dropped on a pinned row, which still makes a folder (spec §A.6 is explicit about pinned-onto-pinned); joining two pinned entries goes through Split With, ⌥-click, the card's drop zones or ⇧⌘=. (4) ⌘W on a pane: a Today tab closes, a permanent entry gets §A.3 without the switch and leaves the split (it stays in the sidebar); × in a pane header is the same; ⌃⇧- takes the focused pane out of the split without closing it. (5) ⌥-click on another entry splits it with the selected one (Zen); on the selected entry it duplicates it into the split (spec); on the group row it separates all. ⌥⌘G/V/H outside a split splits the selected entry with the next in sidebar order (Zen). (6) The grid is two columns over two rows, a third pane spanning the bottom row; ratios for the grid are two column and two row fractions; a new layout or a pane joining or leaving resets sizes to equal; sizes are committed to the store when a resizer is released. (7) The panes share one card, separated by 1 pt lines (spec §D's 1 px resizer), not separate cards; the card's fill follows the focused pane, each pane's own fill its page. (8) Peek covers the whole split (spec §D) and belongs to the focused pane's entry; Peek ▸ Split adds the Peek's page after its entry (a full split refuses with the toast). (9) The Split menu is a temporary top-level menu until 2.8's menu set; ⌃⇧1…4 are matched by their shifted US characters (`!`, `@`, `#`, `$`). (10) The empty pane from ⇧⌘= has no page and shows the card's empty state; the palette over it dismissed without a choice removes it. (11) The 2.7b captures are window captures under `research/screenshots/phase-2.7b/` (the 2.7a checks were captured again there, on the new card). (12) Commits: the carry-over, model and store, geometry, the sidebar, the tab controller, the card and the spike, captures, docs.

  - Done: `Settings.firstRunDone`, `.crashReports`, `.automaticUpdateChecks` in Model (off / not done for older databases). New package `Integration`: `URLInbox` (links before the store and engine queue, then go in order; http, https and file only), `FirstRun` over Settings, `OptInServices` (the updater and the crash reporter are created only when opted in, Sparkle's schedule set before its start; a check the user asks for starts the updater with its schedule still off; no DSN, no reporter), `DefaultBrowser` (`NSWorkspace.setDefaultApplication` for http, then https if the system did not move it; `com.apple.coreservices.uiagent` activated whether running or launched for the dialog; refreshed when Room becomes active), `BuildConfiguration` (the DSN and feed read from Info.plist); targets `Updates` (`SparkleUpdater`: `SPUStandardUpdaterController(startingUpdater: false)`, a DEBUG feed override through the delegate) and `CrashReports` (`SentryCrashReporter`: crash reports only, no sessions, tracing, network breadcrumbs or PII). Sparkle 2.10.0 and Sentry 9.30.0 pinned exactly. `xcodegen/base.yml` now generates Info.plist: `CFBundleURLTypes` http/https, `CFBundleDocumentTypes` public.html/public.xhtml and public.url (Viewer, Alternate), `SUFeedURL` = `ROOM_UPDATE_FEED_URL` (placeholder `https://updates.room.invalid/appcast.xml`), `SUPublicEDKey` = `SPARKLE_PUBLIC_ED_KEY`, `SUEnableAutomaticChecks` NO, `SUAutomaticallyUpdate` NO, `SUEnableSystemProfiling` NO, `RoomSentryDSN` = `ROOM_SENTRY_DSN` (empty). App: `application(_:open:)` into the inbox, opened after the engine profiles exist as Today tabs (`.link`) in the active space, the window shown first when none is; the first-run card (`FirstRunCardView`, an AppKit surface with a SwiftUI leaf) in `SidebarBodyView.accessoryView`; Settings ▸ General (default browser status and Set as Default…, Show Welcome Card) and ▸ Privacy (both opt-ins, Check Now); App ▸ Check for Updates… and Set as Default Browser… with validators; the opt-ins applied before the window and on every change. DEBUG: spikes and fixtures finish the first run unless `-RoomFirstRun YES`; `-RoomUpdateFeedURL`, `-RoomSentryDSN`; spikes `appintegration2` (`scripts/app-integration.sh --session 2.8b`, `research/spikes/phase-2.8/` dev feed and endpoint, `scripts/lib/open-with-workspace.swift`) and `netcheck` (`scripts/netlog-check.sh`, `scripts/lib/netlog-summary.py`); `run-spike.sh` can launch by a link (`ROOM_SPIKE_OPEN_URL`). Tests: Integration 10 (inbox before and after the engine, schemes; first-run transitions and choices; the gates: declined creates nothing, crash reports start/stop and never without a DSN, schedule before start, a user check with the schedule off), Model 86 (settings defaults and round trip extended), UIKitCore 47 (the menu table with the two App items); Room scheme 450 tests with the window tier, all passing; the no-engine CI spec builds and passes with `ROOM_CI`.
  - Numbers (`spike-results.md`, Phase 2.8b; 60 Hz, load 4.6–10.1, light / dark): cold launch by a link — event 294 / 225 ms after process start (queued), page requested 183 / 205 ms later (when the engine profile exists), loaded 73 / 72 ms after that; running — `open -a` 50.6 / 63.6 ms hand-over → event, AppleScript 109.6 / 92.5, `NSWorkspace` 47.9 / 47.8; event → page requested 25.2–27.3 ms, → loaded 25.9–26.9 ms more; after ⇧⌘W event → requested (window shown again) 39.6 / 34.4 ms. Card Done → gone 150–153 ms. Automatic checks on → first feed request 9–45 ms (Sparkle had never checked); crash reports on: 0 requests in 2–3 s. Net-log check (two runs, identical): Room's own code 0 requests, main process 0 bytes in and out; engine: accounts.google.com (`gaia_auth_list_accounts`), www.google.com (`aim_eligibility_fetch`, a preconnect at quit), update.googleapis.com and edgedl.me.gvt1.com (`update_client`), clients2.google.com (`network_time_component`), www.gstatic.com (`ranker_url_fetcher`).
  - Deviations / decisions: (1) One package `Integration` for tasks 3–5 rather than code spread over App: the rules are what the tests build, Sparkle and Sentry sit in their own targets so the tests link neither. (2) Info.plist moved into `xcodegen/base.yml` (`info:`), as the spec asked for the URL types and document types; the file stays committed as xcodegen writes it; AGENTS.md says so. (3) The feed URL, the DSN and the public key are build settings in `xcodegen/base.yml` read through Info.plist (one place for all three); the placeholder feed uses `.invalid`, which never resolves. (4) Sparkle is not merely set to "no automatic checks": while the opt-in is off its controller is never created, so nothing of it runs at launch or quit; App ▸ Check for Updates… creates it on demand. Sparkle keeps its own schedule (`SULastCheckTime` …) in the app's defaults domain; the 2.8b scripts clear those keys before each run. (5) Sentry: crash reports only (sessions off, so an opted-in launch sends nothing until a crash); helper-process crashes (Crashpad) stay Chapter 3 (PLAN §7 risk 10). With the DSN empty the switch is kept and says nothing can be sent. (6) The DSN comes from the build, not the user; DEBUG `-RoomSentryDSN` points it at a local endpoint for the check. (7) Links open as Today tabs with the `.link` transition (history weight 1); file URLs (Finder's Open With on an HTML file) open the same way; other schemes are dropped with a log line. (8) The inbox opens after the engine profiles exist (`startSession`), even when profile creation failed (pages then wait as any page does); without a store (the "Continue Without Saving" path) links are logged and dropped. (9) The card's Done has no Return key (Return belongs to the address field and the palette); the card shows once and is not re-offered after 7 days (`macos-ui-stack.md` §7.4 suggested a second ask; the spec says once). (10) Copy: the card and Settings ▸ Privacy say crash reports and update checks stay off unless turned on; they do not claim Room sends nothing, because the engine still does (B-19). (11) "A link from another app" was checked through three other-process routes (`open`, `osascript`'s `open location`, a separate process's `NSWorkspace.open`); a click in another app routed by LaunchServices needs Room set as default, which only the user can confirm. (12) The net-log check widens 2.4b's method: the update feed, the DSN and the suggestion proxy all point at one logging server, and `nettop` watches every Room process; the summary names each engine request by its Chromium traffic annotation (the hash computed from the checkout's `annotations.xml`). (13) B-26 (location authorization) was named "2.8" in the backlog but is not in 2.8b's spec: left for 3.x. (14) Commits: the Model fields, the package, the app wiring (links, card, Settings and menu in one: they share the AppDelegate glue), the spike, the net-log check, captures, docs.

(2) `GetStatusBubbles` cannot return a forwarding `StatusBubble`: `BrowserWebContentsDelegate::UpdateTargetURL` and `ContentsMouseEvent` static_cast every bubble to `StatusBubbleViews` (undefined behaviour on the first hover); it returns none and the hovered link comes from 0015, the one source; Chrome's loading text is not shown. (3) One `RoomBrowser` per window **and profile**: a Chrome `Browser` holds one profile's tabs and a Room window shows several profiles' spaces. (4) Activation on select uses neither `focus()` nor a protocol call: `//room` activates the tab whose page becomes visible (`OnVisibilityChanged(VISIBLE)`) or takes focus (`OnWebContentsFocused`, a click into a split pane); no public API needed. (5) A Browser closes when its last page leaves, not when the window closes: closing the Browser on window close would close the host's pages behind its back, and the tab controller treats an engine-closed page as `window.close()` (it closes the entry). (6) `OFF_THE_RECORD` is cancelled and logged (no private windows before 4.8, B-50).   - Deviations: (1) `roomApplicationDidFinishLaunching` now arrives before AppKit's `applicationDidFinishLaunching` to NSApp's delegate (was after Chrome's controller), so the host's delegate finds the engine running. (2) `RoomCrApplication` honours AppKit's `applicationShouldTerminate:` contract, which Chrome's `BrowserCrApplication -terminate:` skips (Chrome's controller never answered it). AppKit's quit Apple event asks the delegate itself and waits in a modal loop for the reply: the first build swallowed that reply and every quit by Apple event (the quit-timing scripts, the Dock, logout) hung for 15 s ×3; the reply now goes to AppKit unless `-terminate:` asked. (3) Chrome's close path without AppController's downloads prompt, confirm-to-quit panel and apps-keep-running check (downloads are the host's, 3.4a). (4) RoomCore acknowledges a crash on the host's behalf (a `CrashedLock` released at once) and commits the profile's prefs at load: Chrome keeps "Crashed" until its restore bubble or a tab in a session-restored Browser, and Room Browsers are omitted, so every later clean quit would have recorded "Crashed"; without the commit a kill in the first ~10 s left "Normal" on disk (measured). (5) The isolated world is `ISOLATED_WORLD_ID_APPLESCRIPT`, not a new id: `ExecuteJavaScriptInIsolatedWorld` CHECKs `world_id <= ISOLATED_WORLD_ID_MAX` (content end + 10) and extensions take the ids from content end + 5 at run time; Chrome reserves this one for an embedding app scripting a page and Room ships no Chrome AppleScript tab commands. (6) Blink does not await promises in an isolated world (`PromiseResultOption::kDoNotWait`): `RoomCoreWebContents` wraps the script in `(0, eval)`, parks a promise's outcome in the isolated world and polls every 4 ms; an evaluation unanswered in 30 s is an error (a page with Chrome's `alert()` open hung the tour's `popups` step for 9 min; killed by hand, then `popups` and `keys` rerun with `--only`). (7) The keys spike does not switch Text Input Sources: as in 1.4 it builds each layout's events from the layout data, and AppKit matches menu keys through the active input source (Colemak here), so ⌘T/⌘L dispatch in the active layout only — on both engines; LithuanianColemak is not installed. (8) The keys fixture mirrors its log into `data-keys` (page globals are invisible to the isolated world), the spike adds a typed letter and per-layout reserved/consumed counts, and the tour's keys checks accept a paste at the caret: they had codified CEF's paste replacing the selection. (9) `didConsumeKey` observes the primary main frame's widget only (a focused OOPIF's keys are not reported); a text field consumes the character event, not the key-down, so the key-down still reaches `handleUnhandledKeyEvent` (as on CEF). (10) The quit spike enables `--remote-debugging-port` (Debug) to answer the dialog; with `--no-startup-window` Chrome then holds a keep-alive until the protocol's `Browser.close`, so `RoomAppController` releases it (`ChromeDevToolsManagerDelegate::AllowBrowserToClose`) once a quit passes the point of no return. (11) `didEndMainMessageLoop` resets the `TabRestoreService` of every loaded profile without Browsers (AppController: its last profile only). (12) The embryo host's pages get `HostController` as their `RoomWebContentsDelegate` (unhandled keys to its menu), since the menu dispatch left `//room`. (13) `NSLog` lines of `open`-launched runs were not in the unified log; the quit order was read from a direct launch with stderr kept (`quitstay-2.stderr`). (14) Spikes run without `-RoomEngineDirectory` (`quit-timing.sh`, `tabs-measure.sh`, plain `run-spike.sh`) use the real engine root `~/Library/Application Support/Room/RoomCore` (as since 3.1a); the three hung quit-timing runs were killed there, and later clean quits reset it. (15) The 30 s script limit, the GN deps and the tour-check change landed after the tour; the baseline was rerun on the final build, the tour was not (only `popups` and `keys`).

  - Needs a hand: `research/notes/chapter-3-by-hand.md` "Phase 3.2b" (⌘Q and the Dock's Quit with a page that asks to stay, links and Dock reopen, keys on your real layouts, the crash flags).
  - For 3.3a: `RoomCoreWebContents` observes title, URL, loading and back/forward by KVO on `RoomWebContents`, whose `Observer` (a `WebContentsObserver` in `room_web_contents.mm`) calls `room_refresh` on `DidStartLoading`, `DidStopLoading`, `DidStartNavigation`, `DidFinishNavigation`, `TitleWasSet`, `NavigationEntryCommitted` and `NavigationListPruned` and copies `GetVisibleURL`, `GetTitle`, `IsLoading`, `CanGoBack/Forward`; `didCommitNavigation` can come from the same observer's `DidFinishNavigation` (`HasCommitted() && IsInPrimaryMainFrame()`, with `GetPageTransition()`, `IsSameDocument()`, redirects) or `NavigationEntryCommitted`, through a new `RoomWebContentsDelegate` member. A `NavigationThrottle` has no `//room` registration point: Chrome adds throttles in `CreateAndAddChromeThrottlesForNavigation` (`chrome/browser/chrome_content_browser_client_navigation_throttles.cc:285`), so 3.3a needs a hook patch of 0014's shape there (`room::g_navigation_throttles_hook(registry)`), registering only for Room pages (`RoomWebContents room_existingWrapperFor:`). Discard: Room Browsers are real Browsers, so `TabLifecycleUnitSource` should see their tabs, but a discard replaces the `WebContents` in the strip (`TabStripModelChange::kReplaced`); `-[RoomBrowser room_tabStripChanged:]` handles inserts only and the wrapper's observer would see the old contents destroyed and report `closed` (the tab controller would close the entry), so the strip observer must rebind the wrapper (`WrapperLink`, `Observer`, the key-ack observer, the native view) to the new contents on `kReplaced`. The tour's 3.3a failures narrowed: memory/hidden read `visibility: visible, 63 rAF/s` for pages the host never showed or took out of the card, so `initiallyHidden` and `WasHidden()` when a page leaves the window are the first visibility work (and probably B-53's switching p95); audio's three failures are the audible edge only (`play()` by script runs); tabs' only failure is discard (same-site navigation and ⇧⌘T pass now); Peek never opens because there is no `decidePolicy` (the link navigates in place).

Tab helpers: none of the routes was needed — a WebContents joining a `TabStripModel` gets them from `tabs::TabModel` (a befriended class calling `TabHelpers::AttachTabHelpers`), which every page RoomCore makes for a window already does (3.2a); logged on the first page: favicon, zoom, lifecycle, resource_coordinator, find, session, permissions, translate, print. (1) Navigation: `-loadURL:transition:fromAddressBar:` with the real `ui::PageTransition`, `-reloadIgnoringCache`; patch **0016** (`CreateAndAddChromeThrottlesForNavigation` calls `//room` last; justification in its header) adds `RoomNavigationThrottle` to the primary main frame of wrapped pages, which asks `-webContents:decidePolicyForNavigationAction:` (new public `RoomNavigationAction`, in `room_core.exports`) at `WillStartRequest`/`WillRedirectRequest` with `kind` from `IsFormSubmission`, FORWARD_BACK, `GetReloadType`, `IsRendererInitiated() && WasInitiatedByLinkClick()`; `.ignore` → CANCEL_AND_IGNORE, another tab disposition → cancel and `OpenURL`; pages opened elsewhere (modifier clicks, `target=_blank`, `window.open`) are asked in patch 0014's Navigate() hook (`RoomBrowserWindowClient::AllowOpenFromTab`); `didCommitNavigation` from `DidFinishNavigation` (committed, primary main frame, not an error page, same-document included). `EngineApplication.site(of:)` (the protocol change: RoomCore `+[RoomApplication siteForHost:]` over `net::registry_controlled_domains`; CEF and `FakeApplication` the bundled list, moved from Tabs to the Engine package as `PublicSuffixList`; engine-api row). `PeekRules` reads `kind` only (a link click → Peek; a script navigation, `.other` with a link transition, by the 2 s rule); CEF emulates `kind` inside `EngineCEF` with the status text (B-33's caveat stays for CEF). (2) Page state as KVO properties of `RoomWebContents` mapped by `RoomCoreWebContents`: favicon (`ContentFaviconDriver`), `loadProgress`, `themeColor`, `backgroundColor`, `securityLevel`/`hasMixedContent` (`chrome_security_state`), `audible` (both edges), `muted` (settable), `zoomFactor` (settable, `ZoomController`), with the delegate's `didChangeAudible`/`didChangeMuted`/`didChangeSecurityState`; `Profile.favicon(for:)` from `FaviconService`. (3) `-discard` → `TabLifecycleUnitExternal::DiscardTab(EXTERNAL)`; Chrome replaces the WebContents and `RoomBrowser` moves the wrapper on `kReplaced` (observers, key-ack observer, the view: `nativeView` is KVO-observable and `RoomCoreWebContents` rehosts it); `lifecycleState` from the lifecycle unit; `close()` through the strip with beforeunload. Spikes: Peek kinds and the engine's site key in `layoutmodes`, `zoom_same_host_page` and `-RoomSpikeOnly security` in `chromesurfaces2`, the Web Audio off edge in `audio`, history before the discard, Back after it and favicons in `tabs`, new `closeunload` and `colors` (fixture `research/spikes/phase-3.3/dark.html`).

  - Deviations: (1) No tab-helper route or patch: `tabs::TabModel` attaches them for strip pages; 3.2b's "no tab helpers" was true only for pages in no strip. (2) Visibility needed one more step than `initially_hidden`: unhiding the Chromium view (above); found by the first `hidden` run, where the shown page still read `hidden`. (3) A crash outside the three tasks, fixed: Chrome's ask-before-HTTP dialog (`HttpsOnlyModeTabHelper` after a failed HTTPS upgrade, `http://neverssl.com/`) CHECKed in `TabDialogManager` (no primary Views widget until a first web-modal dialog); each Room Browser now makes its modal host at creation. It existed since 3.2a's tab helpers. (4) `//room` disables `WebContentsDiscard`: Chromium builds take it from the field-trial testing config (an in-place discard that kept every renderer and never replaced the contents); with it off Chrome's desktop default runs and the `kReplaced` rebind is exercised. Even so some renderers outlive the discard (B-56). (5) `close()` uses the strip's `CloseWebContentsAt`, whose `RunUnloadListenerBeforeClosing` runs beforeunload (Chrome's tab close), not `WebContents::ClosePage()` directly, which would bypass the strip's bookkeeping; on RoomCore a "stay" from Chrome's dialog leaves the page alive while the host already closed its entry: the host hears the answer only from 3.4b's dialog delegate (by-hand item 7). (6) `frozen`: M154's lifecycle unit has no per-tab freeze and the protocol no freeze member; `state` shows `.frozen` only when Chrome's own policy froze the page (read at each refresh, no live event), not observed. (7) `decidePolicy` for dispositions other than the current tab rides patch 0014's hook (no new patch); RoomCore cannot tell a `target=_blank` click from `window.open` there (both `.other`), which Peek does not need. (8) `NavigationAction.Kind` gained no case: `.linkActivated` now means a click on a link on both engines and a script navigation is `.other` with a link transition; the CEF status-text workaround moved into `EngineCEF`. (9) `site(of:)` is an `EngineApplication` member (profile-independent), not `Profile`. (10) The throttle also asks at server redirects (CEF's `OnBeforeBrowse` does). (11) The tabs spike's extra history step loads through the engine: a script `location.href` without a gesture is skipped by Back (Chromium's history-manipulation intervention), which read `canGoBack=false` in the first run. (12) New spikes `closeunload`, `colors`, a security check behind `-RoomSpikeOnly security` (real sites over the network, never in a full run). (13) `Profile.favicon(for:)` works but the app does not call it: rows keep the 2.1 cache, now filled from RoomCore's `favicon`. (14) The tour ran palette, tabs and layoutmodes; the `hidden`, `memory` and `audio` checks (tour group layout) ran as standalone spikes. (15) `research/notes/spike-results.md` attributes the tour's activation failures with a standalone rerun; a full rerun was not made. (16) The first push's CI run failed (37257815465): `NoEngineApplication`, the no-engine stand-in the CI spec compiles, lacked `site(of:)`; fixed, and the CI spec built and tested locally before the second push.

(2) The Views modal anchor is ordered above the host's window, not below as PR #1 left it: below, every dialog parented there (print preview) was hidden behind Room's window; the anchor is transparent and ignores the mouse (by-hand item 4 checks that nothing is swallowed). (3) Find's first three keystrokes take ~410 ms: Chromium's `FindRequestManager` delays queries under four characters (Chrome's find bar too); not chased, recorded in the engine-api row. (4) The print check prints `status.html`, not the selected page: Chrome closes print preview when the initiator navigates and the demo's developer.apple.com page changes its URL by script (the preview vanished within 250 ms). (5) The "ps of the Release app over the release framework" could not be taken: `out/release` (3.1b-2, not rebuilt as the prompt says) lacks 3.2b's `-[RoomApplication setApplicationDelegate:]`, so today's app aborts in `main` (two crash reports, two more system dialogs on screen); the check ran on the Release-configuration app over the dev framework (no listener, also with the switch passed). (6) The front-end's scripts: content allows main-world scripts only on devtools: pages, the front-end's CSP forbids `eval` and an injected script has no base URL for relative imports, so `executeJavaScript` inserts the script as an expression there and the spikes import modules by absolute devtools:// URLs; a pending promise whose page navigates now fails at once (it waited out the 120 s limit in the first DevTools run). (7) Release builds drop `--remote-debugging-*` from the engine's arguments (the product never passed it; this keeps a hand-passed switch from opening a port). (8) B-53 got its profile and no fix: the profile names Chromium's hide/show path, the dev build's DCHECKs and two small Room items (strip activation, uncached `isZoomed`), together well under the gap; per the scope rule the number stands. (9) The numbers ran as standalone spikes on fresh states (as 3.3a did), not inside the tour; the tour's own `tabsmeasure` (4,919 MB, 16.93 / 22.62 ms, relaunch 4.95 s) is recorded beside them. (10) Both chrome steps of the tour hung after their end line behind Chrome's own app-modal "Leave site?" dialog (a beforeunload page left by the 2.6a checks, which the host cannot answer until 3.4b); the instances were killed so the tour could go on (found with `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo`, captured in `build/evidence-3.3b/`). (11) `didChangeMuted` was not called for the host's own mute on RoomCore (found by the tour's delegate list); fixed to match CEF. (12) The devtools spike's inspect takes ~11 s on both engines (its own polling; CEF 10.6 s in 2.9); not a DevTools cost. (13) The by-hand list adds the stale "Room quit unexpectedly" dialogs: sessions do not answer system dialogs.

  - Deviations: (1) Six patches, not one: each hook is a separate factory or delegate call with no embedder seam (justifications in the patch headers); the series is 21 (risk 1). (2) Hooks are taken per member: a page whose delegate does not implement a member keeps Chrome's UI, so the embryo host's drives still pass on Chrome's bubble, login dialog, interstitial, Save panel and open panel. (3) The certificate hook sits in `HandleSSLErrorWrapper`, not `AllowCertificateError`: in M154 the latter only denies the main frame and the interstitial comes from `SSLErrorNavigationThrottle`. Go back stops the deferred navigation (no error page is committed), so `RoomCoreWebContents` skips the presenter's Go Back in the same turn (written for CEF's committed error page); with nothing behind, the B-25 empty card follows as on CEF. (4) B-26 is not closed: the grant persists but Chrome reports geolocation "prompt" until macOS authorizes Room's location (no macOS prompt appeared); by-hand step, then close. (5) Close-with-pending-downloads: a quit on macOS never asks (`BrowserCloseManager` defers to `AppController`, which Room does not install), so the host's `applicationShouldTerminate` is the decision: the quit continues and Chrome cancels the download (verified after a relaunch); the `RoomBrowserWindow` member, reached only by private-profile window closes, asks the host (EngineRoomCore: continue). (6) `kDiskQuota` has no protocol kind and is dropped (as on CEF); local and loopback network are one kind; the quiet reasons fold into the protocol's five. (7) Client certificates: no protocol member; Chrome's Views selector stays (B-60). (8) File System Access pickers and a dropped folder stay Chrome's native panels (their own dialog paths). (9) Copy Link Address crashed once in Chrome's toast framework (no BrowserView anchor): `//room` disables the copy toasts; other toast callers remain (B-58). (10) The host's Clear does not remove Chrome's download history: the rows return after a relaunch (B-59, 4.1). (11) `BrowsingDataRemover` requires its observer registered first: the site panel's Clear crashed once (a DCHECK) before the fix, which left one more "Room quit unexpectedly" dialog on screen (the user's). (12) `RoomAuthChallenge.proxyChallenge`, not `proxy`: `isProxy` would shadow `-[NSObject isProxy]`. (13) Both tour steps hung at quit behind Chrome's beforeunload alert and were killed after their end line (3.4b). (14) Context menus keep Chrome's duplicate link items after Room's (Open Link in New Tab, Copy Link Address): the 2.6a merge shows what the engine offers; trimming is Chapter 5.

  - Deviations: (1) No patch (B-61's seam check recorded in the README): `HistoryService`, `TemplateURLService`, `AutocompleteController` are keyed-service APIs, and the focus path and the model are existing `BrowserWindow`/`LocationBar` overrides. (2) The history fixture's visits span 85 days, not a year: Chromium keeps 90 days, and `AddPagesWithDetails` drops older rows, so a year-wide fixture cannot become 50k imported rows; real Chapter 2 history older than 89 days is counted and left out of the import (Chrome would expire it). (3) Chrome's history keeps a reload as a visit (`["link", "reload"]` after pushState + reload); the store's 2.4b rule did not; the tour checks the engine's first visit. (4) ⌘Y lists Chrome's most recent history, not `QueryMostVisitedURLs`: that query scores navigation segments, which imported visits lack, so it showed only the session's 3 pages; `mostVisited` stays in `ProfileHistory`. (5) The import's earlier visits go through a `HistoryDBTask` (`db->AddVisit`, `UpdateVisitRow` for the last visit's transition) after `AddPagesWithDetails`, since that API adds one LINK visit per URL; URL rows that already existed in Chrome's history keep their counts and gain one visit. (6) Suggestions on RoomCore follow Chrome's polling (34 requests vs 3, B-64); the switch still decides per keystroke and off is 0. (7) Inline completion comes only from the engine's default match; with suggestions on it shows on fewer keystrokes (55 vs 72, B-65). (8) `IsLocationBarVisible` made real (true) beside `SetFocusToLocationBar`: `BrowserCommandController` drops `IDC_FOCUS_LOCATION` while it is false (found by the first run: 0 requests). (9) The focus request is a `WebContentsUIDelegate` member on the active page (the `Engine` protocol has no Browser), forwarded by `TabController` for the shown page only. (10) `ZeroSuggest` is not in either provider set: the palette never starts the engine on an empty field, so nothing would ever use it. (11) The `-RoomSuggestProxy` DEBUG flag now also sets the engine's suggestion URL (`http://host:port/complete/search?client=room&q={searchTerms}`, keyword `room-<id>-debug`), so the tour's proxy counts Chrome's requests as GETs, not CONNECTs; the tour check accepts either and wants each query's requests. (12) On a state already migrated (the tour's one state), `-RoomHistoryRows` puts the rows it adds into Chrome's history directly (DEBUG), else palettemeasure would measure an engine without them. (13) The first baseline attempt stopped on stackoverflow.com's Cloudflare challenge (external corpus); the rerun passed. (14) The first tour run (69 / 2) failed on the checks' own faults (spike keys with spaces, an exact count); fixed in the checks and key names, rerun 78 / 0. (15) Omnibox now depends on Engine for the match value types only (no engine object).

  - For 3.7b: (a) **Session.** Room Browsers are `omit_from_session_restore` (3.2a), so `SessionServiceBase::ShouldTrackBrowser` returns false: `SessionService` records nothing for Room pages today (no window, tab or navigation commands), and `TabRestoreService` sees no Room closes. The smaller shape is `//room` serialising each page's `NavigationController` (`sessions::ContentSerializedNavigationBuilder::FromNavigationEntry` → `SerializedNavigationEntry` with `PageState`, back into `NavigationController::Restore` via `ToNavigationEntries`) as an opaque `NSData` per page the host keeps in its store (one getter and one restore member), rather than letting `SessionService` track Room Browsers, whose restore creates tabs in Browsers (the duplicate tabs of the 3.2a tour). (b) **Discard.** `TabLifecycleUnitExternal` (`FromWebContents`) has `IsAutoDiscardable`/`SetAutoDiscardable`, `DiscardTab(reason)`, `GetTabState`, `GetLastFocusedTime`; eligibility lives in performance_manager: `DiscardEligibilityPolicy::CanDiscard` → `CanDiscardResult` (eligible / protected / disallowed) with `CannotDiscardReason` covering `kFormInteractions`, `kUserEdits`, `kPictureInPicture`, `kAudible`/`kRecentlyAudible`, `kCapturingVideo`/`Audio`/`Window`/`Display`, `kNotificationsEnabled`, `kDevToolsOpen`, `kConnectedToUSB`/`Bluetooth`, `kActiveTab`, `kPinnedTab` (it runs on the PM graph sequence: a `//room` query posts there and answers on main); memory pressure reaches PM's urgent discarding through `base::MemoryPressureListener`; Room's own idle timer (2.3) would ask `CanDiscard` before `discard()`. (c) **Translate.** 3.4b's `TranslateBubbleFactory::SetFactory` hands the offer to the host's toast; Chrome's `TranslateBubbleView` never shows. If the bubble is to stay Views, the factory can now fall through to Chrome's for the bubble's later steps: it anchors through the same provider (`GetBubbleAnchor`). The offer needs a Google API key (B-63). (d) **Spellcheck.** `SpellcheckService` (keyed per profile) with the macOS platform checker (`NSSpellChecker`, the system dictionaries; `kSpellCheckEnable`, `kSpellCheckDictionaries`); 3.4a's context menu already carries Chrome's suggestions and dictionary actions in the host's `NSMenu` (`SpellingMenuObserver`); 3.7b verifies the underline on a misspelled word and a suggestion replacing it.

  - Deviations: (1) `navigationState` keeps Chrome's session window (six entries each side, 63 KiB of page state per entry) rather than every entry: the format and limits are Chrome's own; a longer history comes back trimmed to that window. (2) The state is part of `Entry` and so of the JSON snapshot (the snapshot stays equal to the database, the kill test's invariant); at 500 entries with history it is 5.66 MB and a write 19 ms: B-73, not chased. (3) `discardBlockers` is synchronous: performance_manager runs on the main thread in M154 (`PerformanceManager::GetGraph()`), so no `CallOnGraph` hop was needed. (4) It asks `CanDiscard` with `PROACTIVE` (the full list: `URGENT` leaves notifications out) and without Chrome's 10-minute recent-visibility window (the host's idle clock decides when); `kExtensionProtected`, which RoomCore itself causes, is filtered, which would also hide a real extension's `autoDiscardable: false` (recorded). (5) The memory-pressure registration takes `kBackgroundTabLoadingPolicy`'s tag: the tag enum is closed and only feeds field-trial suppression; a tag of Room's own is a `//base` patch. (6) Critical pressure discards one page per second (and per repeated report) rather than "all at once" (2.3's critical): the planner's "until the pressure clears"; moderate keeps 2.3's older half, once per rise. (7) The "PM never discards a Room page" proof is threefold: auto-discardable count 0, Chrome's `DiscardAPage(URGENT)` asked directly discards nothing, and Chromium's simulated critical notification with the host's policy off changed nothing; whether `SimulatePressureNotification` reaches the urgent policy (it listens through `base::MemoryConsumer`) was not traced. (8) Audible pages are kept by the host's own 2.3 rule before the engine is asked (the engine reports `audible` for them too); `recentlyAudible` keeps a page for a minute after the sound stops. (9) Crash recovery restores the selected entry's state and loads it, as the planner asked; spec §H's "restore … but all tabs unloaded" holds for the others. (10) The translate bubble sat over the header's corner with the window at the screen's top-left (its top 23 pt above the site button's bottom), not below the button; recorded, no polish. (11) Look Up's system panel and the underline itself are not verified (the menu's misspelled word and its suggestion are the proof); by hand. (12) Add to Dictionary changes the macOS user dictionary: the spike unlearns its made-up word afterwards. (13) The 50k launch with states used one real 8.1 KB state copied into all 500 entries (the selected entry then loads its own URL with that list behind it), not ten distinct ones. (14) The first discard-revival check in the session run picked a page already at its first entry (`canGoBack` false, correct); the spike now picks a page with history and passed.

  - Deviations: (1) The script re-applies the patch files (scratch index, three-way per file against the old branch's commit) rather than `git rebase --onto`: the patch files stay the source and the branch derived (`roomcore-apply.sh`'s model), the worktree is written once by `git reset --keep` so the build stays incremental, and nothing is half-rebased in the 113 GB checkout during a hand fix. (2) The pin is the one-line `Engine/roomcore/CHROMIUM_TAG` (the spec's alternative: one source of truth for three scripts; `CHROMIUM_TAG` in the environment still overrides). (3) `gclient sync` takes `--revision src@<tag commit>` rather than the checkout script's plain `gclient sync -D --nohooks` (which ran on a detached HEAD): on the `room` branch, which tracks nothing, gclient rebases onto the revision it is given and without one onto origin's default branch; the commit hash also avoids a full `src` fetch. gclient printed nothing when piped (one harmless git error from the gclient root); the script checks the branch did not move, and the four rolled deps were checked at their new revisions. (4) Patches that apply with offsets are refreshed too (0008), not only merged ones: every patch file stays exact against the pin, at one more patch diff per rebase. (5) The run used `--no-build` so 0012's header could be written before compiling; the build was started with the planner's command (log `build/roomcore-rebase-1-build.log`), and the run's state files point `--verify` at that pid and log. (6) `--verify` runs the tour sections in light only by default (`ROOM_REBASE_TOUR_APPEARANCES="light dark"` for both): they were greened on light runs; 3.9 runs both. (7) `--verify` is unexercised (it needs the finished build), and a check of the script's "a build is running" refusal in its real mode was refused by the session's permission check while the build ran: the guards are reviewed, not exercised.   - Deviations: (1) The Archive is part of `RoomState` (written in the same transaction as the entry it replaces, so an archive or a restore is never half done) and **left out of the JSON snapshot** (it only grows and its rows carry navigation states, B-73's cost): a recovery from the snapshot starts with an empty Archive; 4.1b's database backups keep it. (2) A row's id is the entry's: Undo and ⇧⌘T, which put the entry back, take its row out; a restore reuses the id when it is free. (3) Clear's and `deleteSpace`'s routing landed with task 1's store commit, not task 2's. (4) A restored tab counts as used now (`lastActiveAt`), so the next sweep does not take it again (the spec is silent). (5) "The selected" is every space's selected entry in every window plus a selected entry's split partners (on screen with it). (6) Idle = no key or mouse press in Room for 60 s (`Timing.archiveIdleAfter`), checked once a minute after the 10 min are up; the launch sweep runs before the first page loads, where the engine's blockers cannot apply (no page is alive). (7) ⌘Y opens the Library at History instead of the palette's recent-history list (the palette's history group stays). (8) `history-archive.json` rows in the Library are read-only (no ×). (9) A deleted space's pinned entries become rows too and restore as Today tabs (spec §H's Restore); folders have none. (10) `--drop` also removes the series line and the patch file (the by-hand way's first step) and `--dry-run` works for a plain apply too. (11) The tour injects `-RoomClock` two hours ahead; the fixture ages its tabs on the store's clock and the spike moves the clock 13 h for the second sweep.   - Deviations: (1) The member is `removeDownload(id:)`, like `pause/resume/cancelDownload(id:)` (ROADMAP wrote `removeDownload(_:)`). (2) `ProfileDownloads::Remove` stops observing the item before `Remove()`: its last update (an interruption) would otherwise reach the host after the host dropped the row, and `DownloadList` lists an update for an unknown download as new. (3) Restore replaces spaces, entries, split groups, the windows' active spaces and selections and the Archive; profiles are merged (they only grow, B-22), settings stay, the window keeps its id, frame and sidebar (the backup's first window maps onto it when the ids differ). (4) Restore reads the backup into a `RoomState` through a migrated temporary copy and writes it through the store's writer (one transaction, the generation moves on: +2 in the tour, the unloads' flush and the restore), not by attaching the file: the writer's counter and its committed state stay right. (5) "Reloads the window" is the sidebar rebuilding its panes from the store and the card showing the restored selection loaded, as at launch; the `NSWindow` stays. (6) Restore crashed the dev build on its first run (`browser.cc:645 !IsDeleteScheduled()`): every page unloaded empties the window's `Browser`, Chrome schedules its deletion, and the selection's page joined it in the same turn because `RoomCoreProfile` retires a browser only at `browserDidClose`. EngineRoomCore was frozen (the official build had started), so the app holds a hidden blank page per engine profile across the restore (B-81). (7) The launch backup needs a written state (generation > 0), so a fresh state's first backup is the next launch's or 30 min later; the 30-min timer does not wait for idle (the copy is off the main thread, 2–5 ms here); "before restore" backups are pruned by the same schedule. (8) The schedule read as: the newest 10 of today, the newest of each of the 10 days before, the newest of each calendar week back 31 days, the newest of each calendar month back 365 days; a backup dated after now counts as today's. (9) Crash recovery holds only the launch's own loads; the first activation once the profile exists (a click on the selected row, a space switch) loads as usual, and other spaces' selections load on their switch as after any launch. The toast is `Timing.toast` (2 s): by-hand step 9 asks whether that is long enough. (10) The two-space check is a sibling spike (`discardspaces`), and on the release framework it signals the host's `handleMemoryPressure` (the official framework has no spike hooks; the first release run crashed on `roomSpikeAction:`, as 3.7b's `discardpolicy` would: B-82). (11) The snapshot's size was measured with a throwaway Model test on 510 synthetic entries, not on the 50k app state (cheap and the same shape as 3.7b's worst case). (12) Task 8's relaunch check is its own script over the `chromesurfaces2` spike, not part of tour step `recovery`. (13) The archive spike looked the selected fixture tab up by title, which its loaded page replaces; with the Claude app frontmost the page loads before the spike reads (25 / 28 also with the 4.1a app rebuilt from `aecd3c5`); it now matches by URL too. (14) The retention runs after every sweep on the store's clock, not on its own timer.

(2) `equals` ignores case and a leading "www." on both sides and reads a pattern typed as a URL by its host; `contains` trims the pattern; `regex` runs with no options (case-sensitive; `(?i)` in the pattern for insensitive): the spec names only the three kinds. (3) "Most recent space" is the key window's active space, which with one window is the shown space (a comment in `SpaceRouter.mostRecentSpace` for 4.8a), not a separate MRU list of spaces. (4) A tab routed to a space that is not shown goes to the end of that space's Today list as its remembered selection (external opens load it behind the card; page links keep the engine's page), because "under the opener" is in another space; Switch brings it into the card. (5) A page link routed to a space of another profile cannot keep its page (cookie jars do not change under a page): the engine's new page is closed and the URL loads in the target profile, so a popup routed that way loses `window.opener`. (6) Peek's own popups, Open as Tab and Split are never routed (the spec leaves Peek untouched). (7) The tab menu item reads "Add Route for Domain…" in title case like the other menu items (the spec writes it in sentence case). (8) Settings ▸ Spaces is a new section (rule counts per space and "Routing Rules…"; 4.4b extends it), and General ▸ Links has a "Routing Rules…" button too; the sheet opened from Settings attaches to the Settings window, from the sidebar to the browser window. (9) B-35 needed a second change after its first run: with the Peek expanded inside the fullscreen callback, the overlay let go of the page's view before the card took it and Chromium ended the page's fullscreen at once (host events `cardDidShow, enter, exit`; the window stayed fullscreen with no page in it: B-83); `TabController.show` now closes the previous Peek after `display.show`, and Open as Tab selects first. (10) The B-82 guard is one helper used by every spike-hook call (`roomSpikeCommand:` and the omnibox spike's too), not only `spikeAction`. (11) The `routing` step hands external links to `AppDelegate.application(_:open:)` inside the spike (the method AppKit calls; deterministic), not `open -a` from the wrapper; by-hand step 11 does the real `open -a`. (2) The plan's sidebar part is a Model value (`ImportBatch`) applied by `SidebarStore.applyImport`, not a type of the Import package: Store is MainActor-default and does not depend on Import, and the batch must be built off the main thread. (3) Duplicates are counted per target space (its pinned entries and Today tabs plus its profile's essentials, what is there and what the import adds before), so a URL in two spaces of one import stays in both (Arc's "News" in Home and Side; spaces per folder); history is not deduplicated (the engine merges a URL's visits). (4) Links past the Essentials cap are pinned, not dropped (the bar's after the bar's folders in the target space; Arc's at the end of the profile's first imported space) and counted as overflow; the spec says only "up to the cap". (5) Chrome's "Mobile bookmarks" follow "other" at the pinned root; Safari's menu, its top-level folders and the Reading List (as a folder "Reading List") are "other"; a Netscape file's top-level `PERSONAL_TOOLBAR_FOLDER` is the bar. (6) "One space per folder": the top-level folders of the bar, "other" and mobile each become a space in the target profile; loose links go to the target space (made only when something goes in); an empty top-level folder makes no space (skipped with a reason). (7) Arc: a profile is matched to a Room profile of the same name (ignoring case) before one is created; space colour from `customInfo.windowTheme.primaryColorPalette.midTone` only (one colour), icon from an emoji only; split views' tabs join their parent; Arc's own Chromium bookmarks are not offered (Arc does not use them), its history is (`User Data/<profile>/History`). (8) The History fixture has six rows added with sqlite3 after the headless runs: headless Chrome produces only start-up and link visits; the schema and the other rows are Chrome's. (9) Readers keep `javascript:`, `place:`, `chrome:` URLs; the plan skips every scheme but http, https and file with a reason (the readers skip only structural problems). (10) Extensions are read from `Secure Preferences` too, merged by id (recent Chrome keeps them there); a localised name (`__MSG_…__`) is listed raw. (11) The exit's "history queryable through the palette" is checked through `ProfileHistory.search` (what the palette's history group asks) in the spike; the palette by eye is by-hand step 22. (12) The tour step starts from the sidebar fixture `import` (the seed's shape) rather than a fresh directory, so it also runs inside the full tour's shared state; there a "Work" profile from earlier steps is reused (the checks accept made or reused). (13) The spike drives `ImportSheetModel` (pick, options, `performImport`) as 4.2's routing spike drove its sheet's model; the open panel is by-hand step 21. (14) Chrome's `AUTO_TOPLEVEL` visits (start-up URLs) import as `.other`; subframe visits are dropped.

  - Deviations: (1) The ObjC answer is an `NSDictionary` of Foundation values, not `RoomPasswordImportResult`/`RoomPasswordImportRow` classes: a new public class adds `_OBJC_CLASS_$_…` symbols to `room_core.exports` at a place only the official build's `nm` order shows (B-77), and the rules say no new exported class. (2) "Asked once for all" is asked by the host before the import (the sheet's Keep/Replace picker, passed as `conflicts:`); RoomCore continues Chrome's `CONFLICTS` pass at once with all ids or none, and the result says how many conflicted and their rows. A two-call API (report, then answer) would hold Chrome's importer in its waiting state across the host's UI; the policy up front is the same choice made once. (3) B-81's first form (drop the wrapper at `browserWillClose`) crashed the recovery step (SIGBUS: the wrapper's dealloc reset the client `~RoomBrowserWindow` then called), so `Packages/EngineRoomCore` changed once more after the official build had started (`bc97cdc`, Swift only: it is compiled into the app, not the framework; `//room` untouched after the dev build). Reverting would have edited the package as well and left B-81 open. (4) Passwords are a box of their own in the sheet with their own Choose CSV… (another browser's saved passwords are encrypted with its own keychain key; the CSV it exports is the input, PLAN §4 Import), importable with no source picked, into the chosen space's profile or "Into profile". (5) Extensions are off until ticked (the row opens pages), each Web Store one then chosen; the pages go to the bookmarks' target space (an existing one, else the first new space, else the active space); the next page opens on the install or when the tab leaves the sidebar (closed or archived); `WebstoreInstallWithPrompt` (an install-by-id member in `//room`) not taken: outside the eleven members (the planner's decision). (6) The tour's store pages are a local page (`-RoomWebStorePage`, DEBUG) with the fixture's two real extension ids, not a fake id on the real store: a store URL would be a request the tour must not make; the sequence is the same. The install that moves it on is the unit tier's and by-hand step 27's. (7) Task 9: `ImportSources.detectInstalls(home:)` already took the home (4.3a); the injectable part is `AppDelegate.importHome` (DEBUG `-RoomImportHome`). The fallback: `ImportError.Kind.permissionDenied` (a file that exists and cannot be opened) and `ImportScan.needsFileAccess`, also true for a Safari folder showing none of its files (TCC hides them); the open panel opens at the source's folder. Checked with a copied `Bookmarks.plist` at mode 000 (as without Full Disk Access); Safari's real folder is step 26. (8) Firefox: profiles by directory under `Profiles/` named after the random prefix, `profiles.ini` not read; menu and Other Bookmarks both go to "other"; only link, typed and bookmark visits (Chromium's importer's `visit_type <= 3`); hidden pages skipped; keywords and favicons not read. (9) B-84 runs as its own spike (`importlarge`, `scripts/import-large.sh`), not inside the `import` step: 5,200 entries and 50,000 history rows would change the state later tour steps run on. (10) Settings ▸ General gains a section "Import" with "Import…"; the sheet opens over the browser window, which is made key, not over Settings. (11) The sheet is 760 pt tall (was 640) to fit the Passwords box and the extensions list (looks are Chapter 5's). (12) The first-run card's reach is checked through its action (`FirstRunCardView.onImport`) in the spike; there are no card tests to extend, and the click is step 29. (13) The conflict path's "saved" password is imported from `passwords-saved.csv` first, not saved through 3.7a's bubble, so the run does not depend on a bubble. (14) The sheet's tasks 6, 7 and 9 share one commit (`efcf65e`) with the tour step: they edit the same view, model and spike; task 6's sequence and task 8 have their own commits.

  - Deviations: (1) `deleteProfile` deletes the directory itself, not through Chrome's `NukeProfileFromDisk`: that and its retries run at best-effort priority, which Chrome holds until its startup is complete, never signalled by a Room window (177 s measured; B-87); the directory is still marked for deletion, so a crash before the end deletes it at the next launch. (2) "Every `WebContents` of the profile" is the host's pages (made by `-createWebContents` and not closed) plus every tab in the profile's browsers; extension background pages and popups are Chrome's and do not block the deletion (they go with the profile). (3) `contentSettings(ofType:)` lists the preference provider's rules only (the user's and the prompts' answers), not policies, extensions or Chrome's allowlists; a row is changed or reset through `setPermissionSetting` on its representative origin (no member resets by pattern). (4) The deletion's errors cross as an `NSError` in a literal domain: an exported `NSErrorDomain` constant would have joined `room_core.exports` (B-77). (5) B-86's engine-side copy covers the reserved path (a few lines); the unhandled path's retry stays the app delegate's, since the delegate is what offers that key to the menu. (6) The one-time move runs from `main.swift` before the engine starts, not at the app's launch: Chromium opens its last used profile during startup, before `AppDelegate` gets control, so a later move would move an open profile; it reads the profiles read-only from the database and, when it moved something, renames the directories in Chromium's Local State (`last_used`, `last_active_profiles`, `info_cache`'s keys) so the next startup does not make an empty profile at the old path; the marker is the id directory itself. (7) An engine profile not loaded this session (its data from an earlier one) is loaded to be deleted; a deletion has no Undo (its data is gone), the confirmation says so. (8) Clear Browsing Data… has a profile popup (All Profiles by default), because Room has several cookie jars; its downloads kind also drops the host's finished rows. (9) Site data per origin is the site panel's Clear Site Data plus Chromium's own "All sites" page as a tab of the shown space: it renders in a Room tab (the bounded attempt succeeded), but it is Chrome's whole Settings WebUI (B-88). (10) Native messaging hosts (B-69) are not stated in Privacy: Settings ▸ Extensions has room and 4.5 states it there. (11) The statements are one "Notes" section with a row each, not a section each (the Privacy section is already tall). (12) The tour step is a sibling `settings2` with its own wrapper: `settings` already takes two launches, and the rename check needs the wrapper to make a pre-4.4b directory between its two. (13) Tasks 7 and 8 are one commit: they share `SettingsWindow.swift`, `SpacesPrivacySettings.swift` and `ProfilesSettings.swift`. (14) The official build started after the baselines and before the net-log ended (the net-log's 10-minute idle ran beside it, on a cloned app; its first run was lost to relative output paths and rerun); `//room` was final when it started and was not touched after. (15) The Default profile is the first profile (position 0), as `TabController.profile` already treats it. (16) The Settings Section items sit in a submenu of the Room menu, after Settings…. (17) The spelling check's "misspelled" suggestion was not offered today on either framework (macOS's checker); the item list is the check.

  - Deviations: (1) `setSiteAccess` takes a host (the prompt's signature), so the grant is the pattern `*://host/*` (every scheme and port of the host) where Chrome's helper grants one page's origin; the level is checked on `https://host/` then `http://host/`. (2) The `extux` engine section ran on the dev build before the official build started (about 8 min after the baseline): `//room` is frozen once the official build starts, and a wrong member found later could not have been fixed this session; it passed unchanged. (3) `InstalledExtension.directory` (`RoomExtension.directory`, a property on an existing class) is a field beyond the named members, for the details' size on disk; the other route was reading Chromium's Preferences files from the host. (4) "Allow in incognito" is stored host-side (`Settings.extensionsInIncognito`, per profile), not through `util::SetIsIncognitoEnabled`: that is a new member on the frozen protocol and more than a one-liner in the `//room` extensions file; 4.8b applies it to the off-the-record profile. (5) The site panel's Extensions section is SwiftUI, in the site panel that has been a SwiftUI leaf in an `NSPopover` since 2.6b, not AppKit as the prompt said: rewriting the panel is Chapter 5's; the header and the overflow menu are AppKit. (6) The overflow button sits after the pinned buttons (before the domain), not at the header's very end, where the hover Copy button is. (7) "On click" from Room clears the pages' activeTab grant but shows no reload prompt (Chrome's reload bubble is Views): a page keeps what already ran until reloaded. (8) Settings ▸ Extensions' site-access popup appears only while the shown page is a web page of that section's profile; otherwise a line points to the site panel (a host from another profile's page would be the wrong question). (9) The key router runs an extension command before Room's menu also when the key is a Room shortcut (Chrome's priority, and 3.6b's with the page focused); Settings ▸ Shortcuts shows such a command in orange naming the action it takes the key from. (10) `extpopup` was not in the tour (3.6b ran it standalone): added as a step through the same wrapper so verification 5 is a tour step. (11) The tour steps run on their own state and engine directories and count their own minidumps (`crashpad_dumps:`): the tour's state would keep the unpacked fixture for every later step. (12) The fixture's fetch first ran before `sidePanel.open` and cost the command's user gesture ("may only be called in response to a user gesture"); moved after it, 3.6b's side-panel line is unchanged. (13) The Release app `--release` built at 23:24 came from the working tree before tasks 2–5 were committed (the planner rebuilds it with `--release --app-only`; the Debug app over the release framework used by the tour was built from the final code at 23:31).

Unit tier with `CI=1`: Engine 72, Tabs 75, Sidebar 39, Design 9, all passing; the engine-free CI build and tests locally: TEST SUCCEEDED (95 s).
  - Deviations: (1) The prompt's `webContentsDidChangeMediaSession` delegate call does not exist and was not added: `mediaSession` is an observed property (3.4b) and the tab controller's observation already sees each new value; adding a delegate call would be a member beyond the named ones. (2) `MediaSessionInfo.artwork` is PNG `Data`, fetched by Chrome (`GetMediaImageBitmap`) into `RoomMediaSessionInfo.artwork` (`NSImage`), not a URL the host loads: a host fetch would be a request from Room's own code without the page's cookies; it is a field on the existing `mediaSession` member, not a new member. (3) The media row stays above the footer while the Library replaces the spaces view (it is the window's, not the space list's). (4) "× hides the row until the next session" is implemented as: hidden until any page starts playing again or gains a session (a pause then play, a new track, another page); a track change counts as a start because Chrome reports the session as none for an instant between tracks. (5) Row starts are stamped from each page's observation, not in the coalesced row pass: a pause and a play 22 ms apart (the spike's first version) were one pass and the restart went unseen; the spike now waits 300 ms between them as a person would. (6) The play/pause button pauses or plays through the media session only: a page that is audible without a session (Web Audio) is not paused by the row (Chrome's session does not own Web Audio; the row still shows it and a click selects it). (7) No host `MPRemoteCommandCenter` handlers: Chrome's system media controls already fill Now Playing and answer MediaRemote's commands from a Room window (the finding); host handlers would compete with Chrome's. (8) The synthetic play/pause key could not be delivered (posting to the HID tap needs accessibility trust, `AXIsProcessTrusted` false); MediaRemote's `MRMediaRemoteSendCommand(kMRPause)` (dlsym, what Control Center's button sends) stood in for it; the physical key is by-hand step 48. (9) The panel's size comes from `RoomCoreWebContents.requestedBounds` read in App (engine-specific, as 3.2a left it), not a protocol member: the protocol is frozen to the named members. (10) The window server's capture shows Chromium's layers white in the panel and in the main window alike (`phase-4.6-docpip-opener-window.png`), so the panel's content is evidenced by the engine's `captureViewport` (320×180 with the page's text) and by-hand step 53. (11) Dialogs and permission prompts from the PiP page itself are not presented (the panel is its UI delegate and forwards only popups and the close); Chrome's document PiP pages rarely ask, recorded for 4.8's window types. (12) The first baseline attempt stopped at the ten-page fixture with Stack Overflow loading at "ready" (kept as `baseline-1-so`); the rerun passed. (13) The fixture's `currentTime` advanced ~0.1 s per second in these runs at volume 0.02; not chased (the `audio` step measured real output).

  - Deviations: (1) The PDFs setting reaches `//room` as the `base::Feature` `RoomPdfsDownload` through the existing `EngineConfiguration.features` (`--enable-features=…` at launch), not a new `--room-pdfs-download` switch: the host can add a switch only through `EngineConfiguration`, which is the frozen protocol's; the feature needs no member. The settings are read from the database before the store and the engine open (`RoomDatabase.storedSettings(at:)`, read-only). (2) The PDF viewer needed no host fix: find is the host's find bar over Chrome's `FindTabHelper`, which reaches the PDF frame (Chrome's viewer has no find bar of its own either); zoom, print (Chrome's preview, B-29 stays) and the toolbar all work. (3) The toolbar Download's landing is not automated: on macOS 26 the save panels (Chrome's and Room's) are out of process (ViewBridge): `-[NSSavePanel ok:]` raises "not implemented" (the first PDF run crashed on the spike's call; found with lldb) and the folder or name cannot be set while the panel shows; Chrome's panel also starts in Chrome's download pref (`~/Downloads`), so the spike checks it appears with "sample.pdf" and cancels (by-hand step 55). Room's own Save Page… panel is answered by ending its sheet with OK (its folder is set before it shows). (4) Save Page… on a page Chromium cannot save (a PDF: not `IsSavable()`) is refused with a toast instead of downloading the file; the PDF viewer's Download saves PDFs. (5) HTML and complete-HTML saves appear in the host's downloads list (Chrome's `SavePackage` items, which Chrome's download history restores after a relaunch); MHTML saves do not. (6) `sendDevToolsCommand` does not refuse while DevTools is open: the second client attached beside 3.3b's front-end and the capture came back the same (136–143 ms), so no refusal was needed; the member is general (any method), the host uses it only for the full page. (7) The full page is clipped to 16,384 device pixels (`ScreenshotMetrics.maximumCapturePixels`, Chromium's texture limit), not measured beyond 8,000 px. (8) The selection rounds outward to whole pixels: 120 pt at a fractional top gives 241 rows at 2× (the tour allows ±1 px). (9) Open as Tab without a Save first writes the PNG to `<state>/Screenshots/`, which nothing prunes (B-92). (10) The overlay closes when the page's frame changes (a window resize, the sidebar toggled) instead of re-laying out the selection. (11) The Release app `--release` built at 01:20 predates `a15195b` (the save panel's extension fix, the spike) and the docs: the planner rebuilds it with `--release --app-only`; the Debug app over the release framework (the tour's) was built after every product commit. (12) Save Page…'s UI, View Source and ⇧⌘2 are one commit (`a15195b`): they share `DocumentsUI.swift`, the menu table and the palette. (13) The context menu's View Page Source needed no routing: Chrome's `IDC_VIEW_SOURCE` already opens through the new-tab path (patches 0014/0015) under the page, selected; Chrome's "Save As…" (`IDC_SAVE_PAGE`) stays Chrome's (B-91). (14) The dev build loads the PDF in 4.1–6.8 s against 349 ms on the release framework: the dev build's condition, not chased. (15) `RoomCoreWebContents.sendDevToolsCommand` hands the answer through an `@unchecked Sendable` box with a `nonisolated(unsafe)` local: Swift 6 region isolation refused sending the dictionary into the continuation (the local compiler's own error). (16) The `media` spike's `engine` section ran once more (dev) only to check B-90's log line.

The **DevTools member** works with DevTools' front-end open (two clients), any method, one attach per command (`FullPageCapture` in `DocumentsUI.swift` is the caller); **`captureViewport`'s scale** is the screen's backing scale: the image's pixel size is the page view's points × 2 here, and `ScreenshotRules.pixelRect` cuts by the capture's own pixels-per-point. The distiller runs through `executeJavaScript`'s isolated world (the DOM, not page globals; promises polled).

The CI spec locally (`build/ci-local-4.8a.log`, `xcodegen/ci.yml`, `CI=1`): **BUILD SUCCEEDED, TEST SUCCEEDED, 640 tests, 0 failed, 70 s**; GitHub's run: not started (B-93).
  - Deviations: (1) The Today container carries the window (`Container.today(space:window:)`, positions per window's list) and `nil` means the store's default window (the active window in the app, the first window by position in the store, none in tests): one-window callers (palette, spikes, import, links from other apps) keep writing `.today(space:)` and land in the active window; the sidebar and the tab controllers pass their window explicitly. Filtering one shared list per space would have needed index translation in every drop and Clear. (2) `entry.windowID` has no foreign key and no check constraint: a window's close archives its Today tabs in the same write that removes its row, and a stray reference must never fail a commit; the launch adopts Today tabs of no open window into the first. (3) Closing the last window keeps its row (frame, sidebar, active space, the pinned selections) for ⌘N, the Dock and the next launch; its Today tabs are archived like any window's. (4) A quit archives nothing (every window comes back) and moves the key window to position 0, because the key window at quit is otherwise not stored and spec §H loads only its selection. (5) Restored windows other than the front one load their selection when they first become key (they are ordered front without becoming key); a key window always loads. (6) Rows offer no drag operation outside Room: the desktop and Finder accepted the row's URL as a file, so a drop there could never be the drag out (B-97: the URL into other apps is gone, the user's call). (7) The drag out is checked through `debugDragEnded` (the rule, the window at the drop point, the entry moved); AppKit's mouse drag is by-hand step 65, and the row's image slides back before the window appears (B-96). (8) Memory is measured on two fresh launches, not in one run: the first attempt in one run read 4,891 → 5,790 MB on dev with 4,468 MB left after closing the first ten pages (closed pages' renderers linger, B-56), so the second measurement started higher. (9) Renderers cannot be counted per window from the host (no process id in the `Engine` protocol): the pages per window (10; 5 + 5) and the totals are recorded. (10) The losing window's fallback selection runs on the next main-queue turn (the claiming window finishes making its page first) and skips entries on screen in another window, so two windows never take a page from each other in a loop. (11) Per app, not per window: engine profiles (each controller keeps its own map, filled from the app's), the downloads list, `SpaceRouter`, the archive sweeper (it asks every window's controller to sweep its own Today tabs), backups, the extensions center (its one popup presenter routes by the page's window), the site panel, Settings and the import sheet (on the active window). (12) Restore Sidebar with several windows maps the backup's windows onto the open ones by position (the active one first); the Today tabs of a backup window with no counterpart go to the active window. (13) The first-run card shows in every window's sidebar while the first run is not done. (14) The spike renames through the store (the inline field's commit path) and reads window 2's row cell; "in window 1" is the store's change, not a typed rename (step 66 types one). (15) "Open in New Window" sits after Duplicate in the Today and pinned menus and after the Pinned URL submenu in the Essentials menu, disabled without a URL; a folder has none.

  - For 4.8b: **`RoomWindows`' shape** — `App/Sources/RoomWindows.swift`: `WindowSession` holds one window's objects, `RoomWindows.all` the sessions in opening order, `active` the window commands act on, `owners` the `PageOwners` coordinator every window's `TabController.ownership` points at; `AppDelegate.openWindow(record:launch:)` makes a session from a `WindowState` row (`bind`, then `installSidebar(_:store:launch:)`, which wires every per-window piece), `windowWillClose(_:)` is the close path, `restoreWindows()`, `newRoomWindow(_:)`, `openInNewWindow(_:at:from:)`. **Where the move happens** — `TabController.makePage(for:hidden:)` calls `ownership?.tabController(self, willMakePageFor:)` before creating the page; `PageOwners.tabController(_:willMakePageFor:)` asks the holding window's controller to `giveUpPage(_:)` (unload, state saved, MRU fallback); 4.8b's `move(toHostWindow:)` replaces that pair: the coordinator should hand the holder's `Page` (and its reader page, 4.7b) to the claimer, call `contents.move(toHostWindow:)`, re-adopt it there (`adopt` sets the delegates; the page's `profileID` stays) and skip `makePage`, with the fallback selection in the losing window unchanged. **An incognito window** fits as another `WindowSession` with its own `TabController` on the off-the-record profile (`tabs.setEngineProfile` with the twin for the space's profile id, or a separate map), its `WindowState` never written (create it outside `store.windows`, or skip `bind`'s store callbacks), Today entries that never reach the store (a sidebar over an in-memory store, or the session's own list) so `windowID` for them is never written; `windowWillClose` then closes its pages and drops the twin instead of archiving; `RoomWindows.active` and `owners` work unchanged (an incognito controller holds only its own pages; give it `ownership = nil` so no shared entry moves into it).

(4) With no twin Browser in a host window, the hook answers a Browser `//room` makes for the twin in the opener's window (held by the twin's wrapper until it closes) and the host moves the page into the incognito window with the new member, rather than the host creating its window synchronously inside Chrome's navigator. (5) Reader mode ends on a move (the smaller choice: one page moves, the history belongs to the entry's page). (6) One incognito window per profile: ⇧⌘N brings it forward (B-98).
